2,2026-11-12,"Wall Street analysts have seen enough.
The bullish tide is turning for Nvidia as banks issue a wave of downgrades on the stock amid the scandal roiling the chipmaker.
Nvidia was once unassailable, with few analysts willing to even hold a neutral rating. That changed rapidly this week, beginning with a few tentatively downbeat views on the company in light of Huang's affairs, which gave way to a deluge of downgrades on Thursday shortly before Jensen Huang stepped down as CEO. CFO Colette Kress will take over as interim CEO.
The situation has been a nightmare for investors, who had been fretting about Huang's tenure at the chipmaker amid revelations about an affair with a suspected Chinese spy and another relationship with Nvidia insider Kimberly Powell, the company's vice president of health care.
Huang has been the face of the AI since the boom kicked off at the end of 2022, steering the company through an enormous growth cycle that's sent the stock soaring.
Analysts hit the stock with a series of downgrades as the sell-off accelerated. Nvidia, once the world's most valuable company by market cap, plunged another 11%. The stock is down 27% from its peak in early October.
Here's what analysts are saying about Huang's departure:
In a note leading up to Huang's exit, Bernstein slashed its rating on Nvidia from ""Outperform"" to ""Market-Perform"" and lowered its price target from $400 to $200.
In a separate note earlier this week, analysts pointed to the recent New York Times report that unveiled Huang's second publicly known affair with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of healthcare.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
""Where did he find the time? a team led by Stacy Rasgon, a senior analyst at the firm, wrote. ""We've had enough, and prefer to watch from the sidelines.""
Tech bull Dan Ives slashed his price target on Nvidia from $300 to $225 a share. Previously, he said that if Huang were to depart Nvidia, the stock could fall as much as 30%.
Ives, who has relentlessly aired his bullish view of Nvidia over the years, called Huang's departure a ""dark moment"" for the company and the US tech industry at large. Other tech leaders will need to ""step in up even more"" now that Huang, who's been likened as an AI visionary, is gone, he wrote in a note Thursday morning.
He also floated the possibility that Tim Cook, Apple's former CEO, could be a ""dark horse"" candidate for Nvidia's new leader.
""What seemed impossible a week ago is now a reality,"" the Yorkville Ives senior managing director wrote. ""The old Jensen, the black leather jacket, the Godfather of AI, the rock star viewed as the face of the AI boom, is over and in the past; his value to the company pre and post this week is dramatically different, and the board did what we said it needed to do.""
Ives said that Nvidia still remained the ""clear leader"" in AI chips, adding in a previous note that his firm wasn't downgrading shares.
Analysts at Oppenheimer downgraded Nvidia from ""Outperform"" to ""Perform,"" and removed their price target for the stock. Analysts called Huang's departure a ""potentially thesis-changing event' for investors.
The firm also flagged the risk that Nvidia could soon see ""brain drain,"" with its most talented workers leaving on news that Huang is out.
""This 'one-two' punch appears to have forced the board's hand. Huang's role as the 'face of AI' cannot be overstated. His uniquely skillful, forward-thinking, steady and tireless leadership has been critical to NVDA's success in becoming the undisputed AI leader and world's largest company,"" the firm wrote, adding that it believed Huang was ""deeply involved"" in all of Nvidia's strategic decisions.
""We see massive shoes to fill and no obvious successor,"" they added.
Ahead of Huang's resignation, William Blair analysts said his likely exit and ""heightened scrutiny"" of Nvidia would likely be a ""significant overhang"" on the stock over the near term.
The firm downgraded its rating from ""Outperform"" to ""Market Perform.""
""Our concern, though, is that without Huang, Nvidia loses the creative, relationship-driven dealmaking that has underpinned its market leadership over the last two years,"" the analyst said. ""While existing contracts will survive a CEO change, what's at risk is the next round of deals, and with it Nvidia's ability to defend its share into 2027 and beyond.""
Huang's departure opens up Nvidia to more competition from companies like Broadcom and AMD, analysts added.
Beyond Huang's scandal, the firm said it was also concerned that Nvidia's competitive moat around AI infrastructure was narrowing, pointing to growing success around GPU alternatives.
KeyBanc downgraded Nvidia from ""Overweight"" to ""Sector Weight,"" citing the ""long-term execution"" and strategic risks if Huang were to leave the chipmaker.
Huang has played a critical role in operating the firm and setting up its long-term strategy so far, analysts said. In 2024, Huang said he had 55 direct reports, a detail that's sparked fear that the former Nvidia CEO won't be easily replaced.
""We believe Huang's departure represents a material increase in strategic, organizational, and execution risk that warrants a higher risk premium and lower valuation multiple for the stock. While we expect core fundamentals to remain strong for the foreseeable future, we believe it will be challenging for NVDA to find a successor with the same leadership and strategic vision as Huang,"" analysts wrote on Thursday.
The bank removed its price target for Nvidia, but said it saw a fair value of $170, implying another 2% downside from current levels.
Citi slashed its price target on Nvidia to $225 a share ahead of Huang's departure on Thursday.
The bank said it would maintain its ""Buy"" rating on the stock. ""If Jensen were forced out, it would mark the end of an era,"" analysts wrote in a note.
""A leadership transition creates an overhang on the stock given his central role in strategy, product vision, customer relationships, and investor confidence.""

3,2026-11-12,"Michael Burry says Jensen Huang's exit will deal a heavy blow to Nvidia, and investors should sell their shares before the company hits the skids.
Huang resigned as the AI chipmaker's CEO on Thursday morning following fresh reports about two alleged extramarital affairs.
After it emerged on Wednesday that Nvidia's board was considering ousting Huang, Burry — who has been publicly betting against Nvidia for many months — laid out in a Substack post why he expected Nvidia to suffer without Huang in charge.
Huang has ""orchestrated"" Nvidia's efforts to expand the AI ecosystem, Burry wrote. He pointed to its huge investments in AI stocks, and its use of special vehicles and hidden debt to finance customers' purchases of its chips, creating ""tremendous leverage in the system.""
He also claimed that Huang pushed customers to buy chips they didn't need yet by threatening to reduce their allocations in the future.
Without Huang at Nvidia's helm, the ""cash may cease to flow,"" and investments could dry up or be written off, Burry wrote.
Customers will be free to ""slow down deals, push back, renegotiate terms, or simply walk to a competitor without fear of offending"" the chip chief, he continued.
""Nvidia's business will not disappear, but its growth may slow and, critically, its margins may fall,"" Burry added.
""The terms of trade and the architecture of financing were the secret sauce that supercharged an already dominant technology,"" he wrote, adding that it will ""take time for this Byzantine web to untangle and unwind.""

""Nvidia bulls will have another fluffy big chance to get out,"" Burry added. ""Get out.""
Nvidia stock plunged at the start of trading on Thursday and has seen a near-35% drop from its $ 243-per-share peak in early October.
Burry, who shot to fame after his prescient bet against the mid-2000s housing bubble was featured in the book and movie ""The Big Short,"" pivoted from running a hedge fund to writing about his personal investments last year.
He's one of several commentators who soured on Nvidia in recent days.
Bernstein Research's Stacy Rasgon downgraded the stock to ""Market Perform"" and halved his price target to $200 in a research note before Huang's resignation.
In a Bloomberg TV interview, he warned of more bad press and regulatory scrutiny, questioned whether Nvidia could find a CEO as visionary as Huang, and flagged the risk of an employee exodus.
Oppenheimer's Rick Schafer downgraded Nvidia to ""Perform"" and removed his price target in a Wednesday research note.
He wrote that Huang plays a crucial role as the ""face of AI,"" his leadership has been ""critical to Nvidia's success,"" and the company has ""massive shoes to fill and no obvious successor.""
He added that Huang's exit could spark a ""brain drain"" and result in a ""lingering share overhang.""
Nvidia's directors have appointed the company's finance chief, Colette Kress, as interim CEO while they search for a permanent replacement for Huang.

4,2026-11-12,"Jensen Huang stepped down as the chief executive of Nvidia on Thursday, four days after the company announced its internal investigation into an affair he had with Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, an alleged asset of China’s Ministry of State Security.
“Jensen Huang has resigned as President, Chief Executive Officer, and a member of the Board, effective immediately,” the Nvidia board said in a statement. Nvidia’s chief financial officer, Colette Kress, will serve as interim CEO. The board said it “will consider internal and external candidates” for a permanent replacement.
The board’s investigation was announced on November 8, slightly less than two weeks after The New York Times reported that the chief executive had been the target of a Chinese intelligence operation facilitated through his relationship with Alice Huang, who has no known relation to Jensen. The pair have been spotted in public together at least twice, in June 2024 and May 2025. On November 11, The New York Times reported that Jensen Huang also allegedly had a relationship with a direct report at Nvidia.
The alleged relationship with a subordinate, along with Huang’s business dealings with Megaspeed, a Singapore-based AI company where Alice Huang previously held a senior position, likely played a greater role in the board’s decision to oust Huang than the Chinese espionage allegations, a source familiar with the matter tells WIRED.
US intelligence officials reportedly briefed President Donald Trump on Huang’s relationship with Alice last year, but the president did not act on it. On November 6, The New York Times and Financial Times reported that Alice was a spy for China’s Ministry of State Security and that she had been recruited to collect information on the Nvidia executive. (The Chinese government has rejected all claims that Alice was a spy for the country.)
Huang sent a companywide email on Sunday acknowledging his affair, apologizing to his family and Nvidia employees, and pledging cooperation with the internal investigation. The executive has been married to his wife, Lori Huang, since 1985, and both of the couple’s two children work at the company.
On Monday, WIRED exclusively reported that last year Huang began working with a crisis PR firm to manage his personal communications. In October 2025, those PR consultants started to be looped in on all press inquiries involving Huang, whether those inquiries were personal or professional in nature.
The timing lines up with a New York Times report that month that the US Commerce Department was investigating whether Alice Huang’s Megaspeed was helping Chinese companies get around American export controls with the help of a vast web of subsidiaries. The investigation was reportedly halted and transferred to the Justice Department after Nvidia general counsel Tim Teter inquired about it.
Huang has been a vocal critic of these export controls, which he argues unnecessarily constrain the market for American chips, create discrete Chinese and American tech ecosystems, and spur China to build competitive chips of its own. Huang has worked to develop a relationship with Trump in order to advocate for policies that are advantageous for Nvidia, including the idea of opening its markets to China.
Huang cofounded Nvidia in 1993 with friends Chris Malachowsky and Curtis Priem. The trio reportedly brainstormed their vision for the company in a Denny’s diner and aspired to bring realistic 3D graphics to personal computers. For the first few decades of its existence, Nvidia was primarily known in the gaming community for creating chips powerful enough to render high-quality in-game graphics.
In 2012, after a new kind of neural network known as AlexNet won a high-profile prize in computer vision, Nvidia pivoted from high-speed computer graphics to building chips that would support artificial intelligence. Huang has repeatedly said that the introduction of AlexNet, which was developed by AI engineers Alex Krizhevsky, Ilya Sutskever, and Geoffrey Hinton, was a “Big Bang”–like event for computing.
Because its new GPUs proved highly effective at powering AI models that require massive amounts of processing power, the value of Nvidia shares soared in 2023 and 2024, in lockstep with the broader AI boom that began sweeping through the world economy. In the summer of 2025, Nvidia became the first company in history to cross $4 trillion in value.
As of September 2026, the company controlled about 90 percent of the GPU market. Much of Nvidia’s epic rise over the few decades has been attributed not just to its cutting-edge chips and its software “moat,” CUDA, that keeps developers locked into those chips, but also to the fast-thinking, controlling, charismatic, and often bombastic leadership style of Huang himself. Now, that critical part of the company’s success is out.

7,2026-11-12,"Nvidia is without its founder for the first time in more than 30 years.
On Thursday, Nvidia's board announced CEO Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation, ending the tenure of a leader who had overseen the chipmaker's rise to become one of the world's most valuable companies.
Huang's ouster came after days of reports about his personal life. First, The New York Times reported that the Nvidia leader had a close relationship with someone US officials suspect to be a Chinese intelligence asset.
On Sunday, Huang acknowledged having an affair, and Nvidia's board launched an investigation into whether Huang had been targeted by a foreign intelligence operation. The Times had reported that the Nvidia founder had grown close to Alice Huang, who US officials believed had been recruited to gather information for the Chinese government.
Then on Wednesday, the Times reported that Huang had another affair with Kimberly Powell, an Nvidia executive who reported to him.
By Thursday morning, one of the world's richest men and a face of the AI boom was out of a job.
Hugging Face CEO Clem Delangue said, ""This whole thing sucks.""
""I wish none of it had happened. It's a really sad day!"" Delangue wrote on social media.
Delangue, whose company Nvidia agreed to acquire for roughly $12.9 billion, said that he'll continue to fight for open-source AI.
""I want to make one thing clear: we'll keep fighting for open-source AI, harder than ever,"" he wrote. ""This is too important to slow down now. Let's go!""
Tech megabull Dan Ives said that Nvidia's board ""had no choice.""
""Time to look forward for Nvidia without Jensen,"" Ives wrote on X. ""Dark chapter for Jensen and Nvidia.""
Ives also pointed to his earlier statement about what he expects the stock to do without Huang at the helm, which was published before the resignation was announced.
""We see the stock valuation at $225 without Jensen ($300 with Jensen as CEO).""
President Donald Trump suggested a familiar name to take Huang's place.
""NOW THAT Nvidia CEO JENSEN HUANG, who has done a fantastic job, has resigned, there is only one man to take his place - TIM COOK of Apple. A Legend replaces a Legend!"" Trump wrote in a post on Truth Social.
Cook stepped down as Apple CEO in September.
Former Goldman Sachs CEO Lloyd Blankfein waxed poetic about the moment.
""Sad about Jensen. Shakespearean,"" Blankfein wrote on X. ""But as de Gaulle said, 'The graveyards are filled with indispensable men.'""
(While the quote is often attributed to the legendary French statesman Charles de Gaulle, at least one researcher has raised questions about its origins.)
Sen. Elizabeth Warren of Massachusetts, who is likely to chair the powerful Senate Banking Committee, said Huang's resignation won't end Congress' desire for answers.
""Jensen Huang's decision to resign from NVIDIA does not absolve him, other executives, or the company as a whole of responsibility for putting our national security at risk or for any potential violations of our laws,"" Warren wrote on X.
Sequoia Capital partner Shaun Maguire said Huang will ""leave a huge void, with the loss of a: technical, aggressive, founder-CEO.""
""The American AI community needs to work double-time in an attempt to fill this void,"" Maguire wrote in a lengthy post on X.
Maguire said, ""Infidelity is obviously bad,"" but Huang's ""contributions to American innovation and AI leadership cannot be overstated.""
CNBC anchor Jim Cramer, who has interviewed Huang on several occasions, said Nvidia had no choice.
""It had to happen. There's way too much evidence,"" Cramer said on CNBC after the news became official.
Cramer praised Kress who the board named as Nvidia's interim leader.
""I like Colette very much,"" he said. ""I think she's going to be terrific in trying to figure out where the pieces are.
Investor Martin Casado called Thursday a ""Dark, dark day.""
""Horrible decision for the U.S. and the western world,"" he wrote on X.
Ross Gerber said Huang's departure is ""certainly a low moment for us long term Nvidia investors.""
""Jensen has resigned which is sadly the correct action in this case. Nvidia needs to be bigger than one man,"" Gerber wrote on X. ""AI will go on and Nvidia will continue to be the core player.""
Gerber compared the moment to when Steve Jobs stepped away from Apple.
""I was around when Jobs died, and many thought it was the end of Apple,"" Gerber wrote. ""It was only the beginning.""
Gene Munster, a tech analyst and managing partner at Deepwater Asset Management, said in an X post that Nvidia will rebound.
""The unknown is now known,"" Munster wrote. ""This morning $NVDA will gap down ($150 floor) on the Jensen news, then rallies as the market focuses on what actually matters.""
Munster said Nvidia's earnings next Tuesday should confirm his view.
""AI demand is still accelerating,"" he wrote. ""Nvidia GPUs remain the clear winners.""
Investor Sarah Guo said that Huang's ouster is ""a big loss.""
Before the news was announced, Guo described the Nvidia founder as ""one of the most extraordinary entrepreneurs of all time.""
""He's bent the arc of technological progress, and the growth engine of the American economy rests on the shoulders of the company he built,"" Guo wrote on Thursday.
Journalist Stephen Witt, who wrote ""The Thinking Machine: Jensen Huang, Nvidia, and the World's Most Coveted Microchip,"" said Huang ""was Nvidia.""
""It's like the Bulls without Jordan,"" Witt said on CNBC on Thursday, alluding to NBA legend Michael Jordan's retirement after winning six titles in Chicago. ""I don't know how they are going to replace this guy.""
Witt said Huang's range, from ""world-class computer scientist"" to deal-closer, will be difficult to replicate.
""Jensen could take a diagram of Nvidia equipment, an exploded diagram, and draw it by hand from memory,"" Witt said.
Anthony Pompliano, a crypto investor and entrepreneur, wrote on X: ""There is a good chance today is the bottom of the AI sell-off.""
Pompliano added, ""No greater capitulation than the resignation of the CEO of the largest company in the world.""
The prominent VC said the Nvidia drama should prompt founders to reassess their boards.
""Good reminder for all founders to revisit who is on their board,"" Gil wrote on X, pointing to the OpenAI coup and Uber as precedents. He advised founders to reset their boards ""during good times"" to avoid abrupt action in bad ones.
Travis Kalanick, Uber's cofounder who resigned as CEO in 2017, replied: ""I concur.""
The angel investor and entrepreneur argued that founders are not easily replaceable.
""You'd think we'd have learned from Steve Jobs and Travis Kalanick that one does not just simply replace the founder…"" Ravikant wrote, invoking two high-profile cases where boards ousted founders.
Elon Musk replied, ""True words.""
Fridman called Nvidia's decision to let Huang go ""a huge mistake"" and said he stood with him.
Fridman said the move was ""Apple firing Steve Jobs all over again, but much bigger,"" given AI's importance. He said he had stayed quiet out of respect for Huang's family and team and because he personally hates infidelity.
Still, Fridman wrote that Huang ""is human, and makes mistakes like all of us do."" He said his own interactions with Huang had shown kindness and humility, and he pushed back against the ""mass hysteria"" around spy allegations.
Lütke called Nvidia's CEO transition a ""generational blunder.""
Lütke said that was his view ""at least based on the information that's available.""
Responding to a comment that Nvidia lacked a succession plan after 33 years under Huang, Lütke said there could never be a ""true succession"" for a founder like Huang, and warned Nvidia could become an imitation of the company he built.
""There is no replacing a founder like Jensen,"" he said.

8,2026-11-12,"Executive Editor and lead writer for BI Today
Jensen Huang's days appear to be numbered.
That's the sentiment following a report of another affair, this time with one of his direct reports. Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of healthcare, had an extramarital relationship with Huang, according to a New York Times report on Wednesday that cited five company sources.
It's the second report of Huang cheating on his wife of over 40 years, Lori. The first was Alice Huang, who has been an executive for Megaspeed, an Nvidia customer. The Times reported last month that US intelligence officials believed she was passing information to Beijing.
Now, The Wall Street Journal reports that Nvidia's board, which was already planning an investigation into Huang, is preparing to remove him. We've compiled what top business and tech executives are saying about the news.
It's a shocking turn of events considering how a Huang ouster was considered a long shot just a few days ago. Early this week, prediction markets had the odds of him leaving before the end of 2026 below 20%. Now, the bet is less on if he'll go and more on when he'll go.
When I met with Brian Mulberry, chief market strategist at Zacks Investment Management, on Wednesday morning, he put the chances of Huang getting completely removed from Nvidia at 5-10%.
""You have to handicap stuff that's really hard to handicap, right?"" Mulberry told me. ""But he is such a big part of whatever happens next with Nvidia, just like Elon is with Tesla and SpaceX, that it could be incredibly disruptive.""
But exactly how disruptive? And to whom? Let's get into it:
Nvidia: Tech bull Dan Ives previously said Huang's departure would shave 30% off Nvidia's stock price. The issue isn't just Huang's massive profile in the AI ecosystem. There's also no clear successor waiting in the wings. That could force the board to look outside its own ranks.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
Huang: The leather-jacket-toting executive's image has certainly taken a hit. But three decades spent building Nvidia into the king of the AI trade has built him enough goodwill with some. Elon Musk said it would be ""an honor"" to hire Huang if he gets pushed out.
China: In a race, sometimes it's less about speeding up and more about the leader stumbling. That's the current situation in AI. Chaos at Nvidia, a key asset for the US, is a chance for China to close the gap.
The AI trade: AI-related volatility has been the name of the game in the market for a while. But removing arguably the highest-profile executive from his seat creates a different type of turbulence. Or does it? The broader market was up on Monday after initial reports of Huang's affair and spy scandal. Was that because investors thought Huang would survive, or a sign that the wider market's resilience is greater than that of one larger-than-life CEO?
Anthropic's IPO: Entering the public market isn't something you want to do when the water is choppy. One of the world's largest companies unexpectedly replacing its CEO won't keep things smooth. Bonus points if the aforementioned company is also participating in the $26 billion private placement you're running alongside your IPO.
Tech relationships: Huang potentially getting caught in a honeypot could have everyone else in the tech industry second-guessing their romantic partners. (Don't worry, I'm sure it's your personality and not your access to GPUs that she's into.)
Grand Theft Auto 6: Who would have guessed that before the game's long-awaited release we'd potentially get one final submission to its long-running meme: We got a new Nvidia CEO before GTA 6.

11,2026-11-12,"Editor's note: This story was originally published in October of 2025 and has been updated to reflect Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's resignation.
Lisa Su is the engineer behind one of the most dramatic corporate turnarounds in the tech industry.
The Taiwanese American CEO is widely credited with transforming AMD from a struggling semiconductor firm on the brink of collapse into a global powerhouse competing head-to-head with Intel and Nvidia.
When Su joined AMD in 2012, the company's market value was under $2 billion. Within a decade of her leadership, AMD's innovations in high-performance computing and graphics, especially the Ryzen CPUs and EPYC server processors, catapulted the company's value to over $950 billion as of Thursday.
Su's leadership has not been without challenges. AMD's two largest competitors, Nvidia and Intel, are now working together in a strategic collaboration. Under the second Trump administration, export regulations related to China, one of AMD's largest markets, are also constantly changing.
On Thursday, AMD's biggest rival Nvidia announced that its CEO Jensen Huang, who is also Su's cousin, is resigning. As Nvidia searches for a new CEO, some industry observers have floated Su as a possible outside candidate.
AMD's stock was up 5% on Thursday.
Here's a look at the timeline of Su's career, from her early life in New York City to her role as one of the most influential women in tech and innovation:
Lisa Tzwu-Fang Su was born in Tainan, Taiwan, in 1969, to Su Chun-hwai and Sandy Lo. Her family immigrated to the US when she was around 3 because her father, a statistician, was pursuing his graduate studies at the University of Texas in Austin. At that time, her mother was an accountant. The family eventually settled down in Queens, New York City.
As a child, Su liked to take apart and rebuild remote-controlled cars and other gadgets, which sparked her interest in engineering. She eventually attended The Bronx High School of Science and graduated in 1986.
Su attended the Massachusetts Institute of Technology in Boston for many years and focused on electrical engineering. She eventually completed her bachelor's, master's, and doctoral degrees all at MIT throughout the 1990s.
Su's doctoral work on MOSFETs — tiny electronic switches inside computer chips — helped cement her future in some of the world's best-known semiconductor companies.
After leaving academia, Su worked in engineering, research, and managerial roles at Texas Instruments, IBM, and Freescale Semiconductor.
As the lead scientist at the IBM Semiconductor R&D Center, she contributed to the development of copper interconnects in chips, replacing aluminum ones. Her later role as senior vice president and general manager at Freescale, where she prepared the company for its IPO, gave Su the experience she needed to take on roles beyond research.
Su joined AMD in 2012 as the general manager of Global Business Units. AMD's market cap that year was a little less than $2 billion.
At that time, most of AMD's revenue came from traditional chips for PCs. However, the PC market was shrinking in the early 2010s while demand for smartphones and tablets was booming.
AMD began adopting a ""semi-custom"" approach, modifying its core technologies to meet individual customer needs. This approach helped secure contracts to design and manufacture chips for Sony's PlayStation 4 and Microsoft's Xbox One, which reduced reliance on the PC market.
In October 2014, Su was promoted to become President and CEO of AMD.
Su and Huang, former CEO of Nvidia, are actually related. They are first cousins, once removed, with Huang being the elder. Su's maternal grandfather is the eldest brother of Huang's mother.
Despite both immigrating to the US from Taiwan as children, they did not grow up together and had never met until well into their respective careers.
On Thursday, Nvidia said Huang had resigned from his role. It follows an extramarital affair scandal and a board investigation into concerns that he may have been the subject of a foreign intelligence effort.
Huang has also previously joked about his rivalry with Su. At an all-hands meeting last year, he recalled how his parents had sent him and his brother to live with an uncle related to Su in the US before they enrolled in a Kentucky reform school, where he was assigned to clean bathrooms.
He blamed the ordeal on Su's side of the family, saying they'd been ""out for blood"" since he was nine
""When we give a presentation, the whole market goes up,"" he joked. ""When she gives a presentation, my stock price goes down.""
In 2014, AMD had a large but scattered portfolio of chips for PCs, embedded systems, graphics, and low-power mobile, but the lack of focus was causing the company to lose market share to Nvidia and Intel.
In a change of direction for the company, Su decided to discard the market of low-power chips with small margins to focus on producing high-end chips for the cloud, data centers, and consoles.
The development of the Ryzen chips helped revive its market share among consumer products, while the simultaneous launch of the EPYC processor chips helped carve out its share among data centers and servers. Companies with high demand for cloud services, such as Amazon, Microsoft, and Google Cloud, have all been adopting EPYC chips.
In 2018, AMD also launched the world's first 7-nanometer data center GPUs, namely the Radeon Instinct MI50 and MI60.
By 2020, AMD's market cap had reached over $110 billion.
Before Su started gracing magazine covers, she went viral in a clip from the 2018 Chinese Grand Prix, where Formula 1 commentator Martin Brundle yelled, ""Excuse me, Ma'am, do you speak English?"" at the CEO, who was sponsoring the event.
""I'm with AMD,"" Su said, a response that immediately gained her the internet reputation of being humble. The original clip on YouTube has garnered more than 750,000 views and continues to circulate on Instagram Reels as an example of humility.
In 2022, Su became the first MIT alum to make a gift for a building that will bear her own name. Originally called Building 12, the home of MIT.nano was renamed the Lisa T. Su Building.
Su also regularly appears at MIT's doctoral commencement ceremonies and established the Lisa Su Fellowship Fund, which supports female graduate students at MIT who have made accomplishments in nanotechnology.
Su's work at AMD was recognized by Time magazine in 2024, which named her CEO of the Year. She became the first woman to receive this title.
AMD's business has grown more than a hundred times since Su took the wheel, though it is still a fraction of the size of the nearly $4 trillion Nvidia.
When asked if she thinks she could outcompete Nvidia, Su told Wired in an interview last year that she doesn't necessarily want to be compared to Intel and Nvidia, because ""the market is humongous"" and she sees plenty of opportunities.
Last year, Intel and Nvidia announced a strategic collaboration to develop AI infrastructure and personal computing products, sending Intel shares up 23% as of market close that day and denting AMD's stock. The collaboration is anticipated to create significant pressure on AMD by combining Nvidia's AI expertise with Intel's hardware.
In March 2025, a team of analysts from SemiAnalysis published a report stating that AMD's graphics processing unit is still inferior to Nvidia's because AMD's existing software prevents the chip from reaching its full potential.
Su was not angry. Instead, she hopped on a 90-minute call with SemiAnalysis's lead analyst the next day to hear him out.
""Feedback is a gift even when it's critical,"" Su wrote in a post on X after the call, ""We have put a ton of work into customer and workload optimizations, but there is lots more we can do to enable the broad ecosystem.""
Similar to Nvidia's H20 chips, AMD has developed chips specifically for the Chinese market in response to past US export control regulations, namely the MI308 AI chips.
In April 2025, AMD estimated it would take an $800 million revenue hit after Trump announced more stringent chip export controls that would prevent MI308 from reaching its intended market.
""We lead today because we have the best technology. However, if we're not able to fully have our technology adopted in the rest of the world, there will be other technologies that will come to play,"" said Su in a US Senate hearing in May 2025 regarding export controls. ""They may not be as good as we are today, but frankly, usage really spurs innovation.""
In July 2025, the restriction was lifted, and AMD said it would resume compliant shipments to China. By September 2025, both Nvidia and AMD agreed to share 15% of their revenues from chip sales to China with the US government, in exchange for maintaining their export licenses.
In September 2025, Su attended an AI task force meeting at the White House, led by first lady Melania Trump, and subsequently attended a dinner event hosted by Trump alongside some of the biggest names in tech, including Microsoft cofounder Bill Gates, Apple CEO Tim Cook, and Meta CEO Mark Zuckerberg.
Su had previously endorsed the Trump administration's AI Action Plan, though she was not a member of a formal advisory board. In a September 2025 interview with Fox Business, Su said the AI Action Plan is an ""excellent blueprint for what it takes for America to lead.""
The AI Action Plan has faced criticism for attempting to override state-level AI governance and withholding AI infrastructure funding from states with more stringent regulations.
In October 2025, AMD announced it had agreed to a multi-billion-dollar partnership to supply its artificial intelligence chips to OpenAI.
The move gives AMD a significant boost against Nvidia.
The multi-year deal, announced Monday, will see OpenAI deploy 6 gigawatts of AMD GPUs.
It gives OpenAI the opportunity to take a roughly 10% stake in AMD, with shares vesting as the companies hit deployment targets and AMD hits share price targets.
""This partnership is a major step in building the compute capacity needed to realize AI's full potential,"" Sam Altman, cofounder and CEO of OpenAI, said.
In September, Su attended a White House dinner hosted by President Donald Trump for China's leader, Xi Jinping. She sat with other tech executives, including Huang and Apple chair Tim Cook.
Su leads a very private life outside her role at AMD. Her spouse is Daniel Lin, but it is unclear when the pair got married or if they have children. There are no verifiable records of Lin's profession, and he is not a public figure, though the pair often appear together at public events.
Su told Wired what keeps her going: She boxes with a trainer at her house for exercise, enjoys a good passion tea lemonade from Starbucks, and gets between five and six hours of sleep every night — seven on weekends.

A rear screen, solid specs, and... some kind of heart rate sensor?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Honor has officially lifted the curtain on its next batch of mid-range phones in China, where buyers can now place a deposit for the upcoming Honor 700S, Honor 700, and Honor 700 Pro.
If the company’s previous releases are any indication, the budget-oriented 700S is unlikely to see the light of day in the West, but the more premium 700 and 700 Pro could hit shelves in the coming months.
And, honestly, I’m hoping that they do, because both models are set to bring some impressively high-end features to a market segment otherwise dominated by boring-looking slabs like the Oppo Reno 16, Xiaomi 17T, or Google Pixel 10.
With that in mind, here are the three reasons the Honor 700 is worth looking forward to — and one reservation that could put a damper on the global launch...
 



You might think cool rear displays are exclusive to high-end flagships like the Xiaomi 18 Pro Max, but Honor is set to bring them to a cheaper price point with the Honor 700 series. All three models come with a small color display on the rear, and the brand has already been showing off what you can do with them in a flurry of posts on the Chinese social media site Weibo.
This includes the ability to play some cute little time-killing mini-games or unwind by tapping it to rotate a virtual wooden meditation bracelet. It also has some more practical applications, like reading your notifications at a glance without having to pick up the phone when it’s face down on your desk, or seeing a live preview of the rear camera when you’re shooting photos for easier posing. These aren’t exactly brand-new features in the world of phones with rear screens, but the idea of them being available at a more affordable rate is certainly appealing.
For its part, Honor does seem to have some innovation up its sleeve too. One video shows how easy it is to change its appearance with your own pictures, emojis, or GIFs. A new option appears when you hold your finger down to activate the Honor Magic Portal feature. Drag an image to it, and it’ll appear on the rear screen, which seems like a very Gen-Z approach to customization.
Get the latest news from Android Central, your trusted companion in the world of Android
I can already imagine quickly tweaking it to reflect my mood, or show off the latest TV show I’m watching or game that I’m playing.
 



There’s another surprising feature buried here as well, revealed in a recent image post. It appears as though the phone will be able to read your heart rate and blood oxygen level, conveying it with a little graphic on the rear display.
It’s not exactly clear how it will do this, but there’s most likely some kind of sensor hidden somewhere on the exterior of the device that you’ll have to hold for a few seconds in order to get a reading.
Smartwatch users might shrug, but as an analog watch wearer who relies on their phone for step tracking and logging exercise routines, I think it's a pretty cool addition that gives you access to some more health data without the need for a standalone accessory.
 



Honor hasn't officially revealed the specs of any of the 700 series phones just yet, but the folks over at GSMarena have put together a 700 Pro spec sheet based on the latest leaks from internet tipsters — and it's certainly promising.
It looks like a very well-considered follow-up to the already excellent Honor 600 Pro, maintaining the same glass back and aluminum frame build that made that phone feel impressively premium despite its less-than-flagship price.
The main screen will seemingly be a very similar AMOLED panel, with the same smooth 120Hz refresh rate and incredible 8,000 nits peak brightness to ensure legibility in even the harshest of lighting conditions.
 



The camera setup appears to be identical too, with a 200MP main shooter, 50MP telephoto lens, and a 12MP ultrawide on the rear, and the same 50MP selfie shooter on the front. I used the Honor 600 Pro as my daily driver for multiple weeks and couldn’t fault its camera performance given its market position. It offered poppy, bright pictures that were perfect for a social media feed, so I can understand Honor not wanting to shake things up this time around.
That said, I am disappointed that the phone will still apparently ship with a USB 2.0 connector, meaning that it will suffer from very slow file transfer speeds if you hook it up to a PC to export all of your pics.
The biggest improvements will likely be to the SoC, with the more recent Qualcomm SM8845 Snapdragon 8 Gen 5 replacing the Qualcomm SM8750-AB Snapdragon 8 Elite for a solid performance boost.
The battery is also getting an upgrade, coming in at 9,000 mAh rather than the 7,000 mAh (or 6,400 mAh in Europe) of the last model for even longer-lasting performance — very promising given I was able to squeeze multiple days of use out of the 600 Pro in my testing. Of course, whether markets outside of China get lower capacities is yet to be seen.
The real elephant in the room here is price, as it could make or break the Honor 700 series as a package.
I’m hoping that Honor will be able to maintain the price point of the 600 line (which would help explain the overall rather similar specs), though this might be tricky given the huge hike in costs of key components like RAM and flash storage.
The Honor 600 Pro retailed for £899.99 in its 12GB of RAM and 512GB storage configuration, though it is almost perpetually discounted to around £599.99, where it represents pretty incredible value. The base Honor 600, meanwhile, retails for £549.99, though, again, it is frequently on sale for £449.99.
If the 700 series is available for the same rates, I could see it being a seriously tempting proposition when put up against major flagships. I'll be following the China launch closely.

Jensen HuangNvidia 
Jensen Huang founded Nvidia back in 1993 and called each of the company’s major shots along the way, from its focus on gaming graphics to its latest pivot to AI. He’s now resigned as CEO following reports of multiple affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy.
“Board of Directors announced today that Jensen Huang has resigned as President, Chief Executive Officer, and a member of the Board, effective immediately,” reads a new press release from the tech giant on November 12. Colette Kress, Nvidia’s current chief financial officer, has been named as interim CEO to replace Huang while the board of directors initiates a search for a permanent replacement. Investor Harvey Jones has been appointed chair of the board following Huang’s departure.
“The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the companyâs operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption,” Jones said in a statement. Kress said she’ll build on the “extraordinary” foundation left by Huang and focus on serving the company’s customers.
“Jensenâs contributions to Nvidia are profound,” the press release stated. “Nvidia is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry.”
Huang’s resignation comes after a report in The New York Times of a second extramarital affair, this time with Nvidia’s VP of healthcare technology. Earlier in the week, the 63-year-old executive admitted to a separate romantic relationship with an alleged Chinese spy. Nvidia’s stock price has shed hundreds of billions in the intervening days, and the company faces at least one lawsuit from shareholders.
Once a humble graphics card company lobbying video game console makers for contracts to supply them with chips, Nvidia has become the epicenter for the current boom in AI tech, rising to a multi-trillion-dollar market cap in just a couple of years as it shifted from servicing the PC gaming market to feeding an insatiable demand for new data center buildout.

16,2026-11-12,"RIP, Nvidia under Jensen Huang.
Several bouquets of flowers and at least one candle were photographed outside Nvidia's Santa Clara headquarters on Thursday after Huang resigned as CEO.
The founder and longtime chief executive resigned Thursday morning in the midst of a scandal involving one confirmed extramarital affair and two reported others, and a board investigation into whether he was the target of a foreign intelligence operation.
On Thursday, several bundles of flowers sat against the Nvidia company sign in front of the company's headquarters. A man was also photographed adding a white candle in a glass jar in front of the sign.
The apparent tributes to the tech executive highlight just how influential Huang had become as he helped Nvidia grow into one of the world's most valuable companies and a leader in the AI boom.
Huang and his signature leather jacket had become largely synonymous with Nvidia, and he was one of the most visible figures in the AI revolution.
Nvidia's future without Huang remains uncertain. Colette Kress, Nvidia's CFO, was announced as interim CEO while the company searches for a replacement.
As of Thursday, the stock was down 13% since Friday's market close, before Huang's admission of infidelity and the board's investigation were announced.
Following Huang's resignation, leaders in the business and tech world weighed in on what his absence will mean for Nvidia.

17,2026-11-12,"Author of the First Trade newsletter
There are video game companies, and then there's Take-Two Interactive.
The company morphed into an entertainment unicorn of sorts with the 2013 video game GTA V, which shattered sales records upon release.
But GTA V's real success — and what truly set Take-Two apart — came in the 13 years that followed. GTA Online became a thriving hub for players to explore the game, spend on virtual currency, and buy add-on content.
This has, understandably, pushed expectations skyward for GTA VI, the series' latest installment, which is due out on Nov. 19. Investors don't just want monstrous first-week sales. They're looking for Take-Two's next GTA Online-style annuity — something that can keep paying them for years.
To be clear, Take-Two makes other products, including the NBA 2K series and a full mobile suite through Zynga. But for the company's stock, GTA VI is the only game in town right now (pun intended).
The chart below shows Take-Two stock essentially living and dying with each subsequent GTA VI update.
Shares are up 26% since the start of 2025, trailing the S&P 500's 30% return over the period. Take-Two was actually outpacing the index until the fall from July record highs, though a sharp rally in recent days has helped close the gap.
Share volatility or not, Wall Street is unanimously bullish on Take-Two. All 33 analysts tracked by Bloomberg have a ""buy"" rating on it, and the average 12-month price target calls for 26% upside from Wednesday's close.
A large swath of analysts reiterated their views after Take-Two said GTA VI presales were ""far in excess"" of expectations. Outside estimates put preorders at more than 7 million, worth roughly $750 million in sales.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
Investors — and Wall Street's sell side — were willing to overlook a decline in quarterly bookings and disappointing sales of NBA 2K27 to focus on GTA VI.
Still, a major question mark is when Take-Two will expand GTA VI — which is launching with just a single-player mode — into the multiplayer experience players have come to expect. And how will that fit into its existing GTA+ subscription service?
Wall Street can't accurately model that piece yet, but it may determine whether the game lives up to mammoth expectations.
GTA VI's broader impact is complicated. No other franchise is quite like it, and the 13-year gap between installments makes it an unusually concentrated event.
It could still lift PlayStation and Xbox console sales into the holidays as gamers upgrade. But Sony and Microsoft are too diversified to be true GTA VI-linked stocks. With EA now private, Take-Two is the rare large US-listed gamemaker whose valuation hinges largely on one blockbuster.
The pessimistic view is that GTA VI takes a bite out of rival publishers. Gamers have finite time and money, which is why studios have spent years giving GTA launch windows plenty of room.
In the end, Take-Two is not a one-game company. But it is a one-game stock right now. In exactly one week, we'll start finding out whether its next GTA era can be as lucrative as the last.

18,2026-11-12,"There is renewed attention on the prime minister's push for closer relations with Europe - and that is causing ripples in his own party and others.
Andy Burnham told his Cabinet earlier this week: ""I firmly believe we have ended up with a harder Brexit than people voted forâ¦ no sensible person thinks the deal negotiated by the Tories is working for the country.""
It comes ahead of a week which will focus minds on the UK's relationship with the bloc. On Thursday, at a gathering of European leaders in Dublin, Burnham will for the first time meet those who could prove pivotal as he sells his vision for Britain's future in the bloc.
And next week, he will deliver a speech on foreign policy, in which â for Burnham â Europe is a central plank. He will then travel to Brussels for a UK-EU summit to sign new agreements on food, energy and youth mobility.
Talking about the new deal to be signed at the summit, Burnham said to ministers: ""We will take opportunities that make people in this country better off. 
""There is a significant prize.""
Brexit referendum 'possible' in Labour election manifesto, says Burnham
UK and EU agree draft deal to scrap Brexit red tape over food exports
This lies behind the prime minister's decision to open the door to the argument about rejoining the EU single market - along with the freedom of movement that would bring - and even the EU altogether.
But that longer-term ambition is dividing his cabinet and his party. 
Some welcome his approach, including those with formerly Brexit-backing constituencies. Angela Rayner, for example, is understood to have been pushing former Prime Minister Sir Keir Starmer to be bolder on Brexit since at least autumn 2022.
The housing secretary is said to have been insistent that Labour needs to stick to its manifesto red lines â not to return to the EU single market, customs union, or freedom of movement â but is open minded to changing those at the next election.
Another cabinet minister said: ""The best thing you can do in politics is tell the truth. And the truth is that Brexit has been hugely damaging to Britain. 
""We are not leading a public conversation here, we are racing to catch up with the public.""
But others are watching nervously at a strategy one former minister described as ""high risk"".
Home Secretary Shabana Mahmood, for example, has struck a more cautious tone warning about the ""costs and consequences"" of rejoining the EU as well as staying out.
Jonathan Reynolds, the Business and Trade Secretary, is supportive of getting closer to the EU and discussing the UK's long term relationship. 
But he has previously warned against joining the EU's customs union and is also unsure about the prospect of rejoining the EU, highlighting the uncertainty of what terms the UK would face, and what the bloc could look like in years to come with nationalist parties in the ascendancy in several European countries.
A voice from the past has a different set of concerns. 
Lord Cameron told Bloomberg's Mishal Husain that starting a conversation about rejoining would be ""very long and painful"". 
The former prime minister warned ""flirting with rejoining"" could also harm Burnham's attempts to renegotiate the Conservative's Brexit deal.
""If you start endlessly producing this idea that we're actually on a path to rejoining, I think you're in danger of getting a less good partnership deal because of course the EU countries will just think: 'Well, these guys are gonna rejoin anyway, so what's the point in giving them a good partnership deal'.""
Attitudes in Labour towards the prime minister's approach don't divide along predictable lines. One Scottish Labour MP, personally opposed to Brexit and with a heavily Remain-voting constituency, said it was a ""real problem"".
""How can I argue to my constituents that we had a Scottish referendum in 2014 that settled independence for a generation. but then say the 2016 referendum isn't settled? My view is you accept democracy.""
For now though, that opposition hasn't broken out into public rebellion, although Labour MP Gareth Snell is one who has warned about reopening the debate.
The MP for Stoke-on-Trent, where the Leave vote was 69.4%, told the BBC's Westminster Hour that doing so would ""monopolise an awful lot of time inside parliament, inside government"" when there are other things MPs should be doing.
Two Labour MPs among those most unhappy about Burnham's openness to Europe insisted ""there are no plans"" for any organised criticism of the prime minister.
Despite their concerns, particularly about free movement, they described their current mindset as ""wait and see"". That temporary truce is likely to fracture if the prime minister's lead in the polls begins to slide.
Badenoch has pledged that a future Conservative government will withdraw the UK from the European Convention on Human Rights (ECHR)
But Burnham's strategy has left other parties struggling to react.
The Conservatives have strongly opposed any move to align with EU rules into the future, with the party leader Kemi Badenoch saying that would be a red line and ""actually take us backwards"".
But some Conservative MPs are beginning to express unease about the strategic dilemma presented for their party by Burnham's moves on Europe, fearing that the issue could again divide Conservative voters.
In recent months Badenoch and her team have intensified efforts to win back affluent voters, especially in the south of England, who view Burnham's economic offer as too left-wing.
""The problem is that some of those voters are only just giving us a hearing again after Brexit,"" one Conservative MP said, ""and now they are going to keep being reminded of it.""
The Liberal Democrats have enjoyed being the most pro-European of the largest parties in Westminster. But some Lib Dems now fear their distinct position risks being drowned out by Burnham.
Reform UK though say they are ""very bullish"" about the return of Brexit to political debate, despite polling that suggests public sentiment has shifted since 2016.
""If he [Burnham] wants to fight an election on a Â£40bn bill to rejoin, open door immigration, and less control over our laws and our courts then we are very up for that,"" a Reform UK strategist said. 
The party believes that just as it would build a progressive anti-Brexit coalition around Labour, it would also unite Brexit supporters around Reform UK as the most anti-EU party.

19,2026-11-12,"By 


      Stephan Bisaha
    


                Home sales fell to their lowest since 2010, a new report shows, in large part due to high mortgage rates.
                
                    
                    Mario Tama/Getty Images
                    
                
hide caption

The housing market has finally broken out of the stagnant holding pattern it's been stuck in for more than four years – just not in the right direction.
Existing home sales in October fell to 3.75 million, making an 8.8% decline in sales from a year earlier, according to the National Association of Realtors. The last time sales were this low was 16 years ago.
""It was a punch in the gut,"" said Joel Berner, senior economist at Realtor.com. ""We expected to see a soft month but not the softest month since 2010.""
The drop is mainly due to high mortgage rates.
In October, the average rate for a 30-year fixed rate mortgage hit as high as 7.40%, the highest since 2023. Rates have gone down a bit since then, but are still well above where they were a year ago.
Higher inflation and bond yields have driven up mortgage rates, but some of this housing slowdown was by design.
The Federal Reserve raised its benchmark interest rate in September to increase the cost of borrowing, including home mortgages. Fewer home sales and other purchases mean less economic activity, which the Fed wants in order to reach its goal of lowering inflation.
Policymakers at the Fed have signaled another rate hike could come in December, which could further slow down the housing market.
""It might seem like kicking the housing market while it's down right now for the Fed to continue a rate raising cycle but in the long run getting inflation under control is really the goal,"" Berner said.

20,2026-11-12,"Corporations can benefit the states where they're headquartered, including through job prospects, tax dollars, and other impacts on a state's economy. But which companies do this best?
Taking a look at the market cap, or the market value of a company's outstanding shares, can give residents a good idea of which companies are the most valuable in their states. Big-name companies may dominate a particular state's economy, while popular industries take the lead in others.
So what companies dominate where you live? Check out which industries have taken over the mapâor find the single company dominating your home stateâbased on October 2026 data from Stock Analysis.
Four of the top five companies that have the highest market cap by state are in the technology space.
Nvidia led the way with a market cap of $5.7 trillion in October 2026, making it the biggest market cap of any company and the most valuable company in California, according to Visual Capitalist. But there are several tech companies with similar market caps based in California, such as Apple and Google, so stock price changes could affect Nvidia's lead in the state.
Microsoft came in second place among the most valuable companies by state, leading all companies in Washington. The software company has an October market cap of $3.9 trillion. But like California, the state has several tech companies like Amazon that would dominate in other states but are beaten out when faced with tough competition.
In third place was SpaceX, pushing Texas into the top five states with the biggest market caps. The tech company is worth $2.3 trillion.
Finance is also a big industry for states with high-value companies headquartered within their borders.
It may not be surprising to see New York make it to the top 10 states, with the biggest company having the biggest market cap with a finance company. New York City is known as a financial capital of the world with several major banks and financial firms based there. JPMorgan Chase beats all of them with a market cap of $876 billion in October 2026.
What may be surprising is that New York ends up seventh of the list of the most valuable companies by state with Nebraska ranking in fifth place with another financial company. Warren Buffett, nicknamed the Oracle of Omaha, still has his company Berkshire Hathaway headquartered in Nebraska. The company was valued at $1.1 trillion in October 2026, putting it two spots above New York.
Some states may be best known for the consumer companies that call them home and boost their rankings with big market caps.
Arkansas, for example, made the top 10 states with the most valuable companies because it's home to Walmart, which has an $858 billion market cap. The top 15 also included Georgia, with its deep connection to Coca-Cola, and Ohio, home to consumer goods giant Procter & Gamble. Connecticut barely missed the cut, coming in 16th thanks to Philip Morris International and its $300 billion market cap.

23,2026-11-12,"Tech giant’s market value drops below $4 trillion after Huang steps down following espionage and affair allegations.
Save
Share
Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of tech giant Nvidia following his reported affairs with a suspected Chinese spy and a company executive.
The firm’s market value fell below $4 trillion for the first time since July 2025 following Thursday’s announcement, capping a volatile week as Huang’s scandals rocked the company, which supplies most of the chips powering advanced artificial intelligence (AI).




The company appointed Nvidia Chief Financial Officer and Executive Vice President Colette Kress as interim CEO on Thursday and named its first-ever board chair, Harvey C Jones, who has been a venture investor for the last three decades.
Nvidia’s board met on Wednesday night to discuss ousting Huang, whose relationship with an alleged Chinese spy triggered investigations by the FBI, the United States Congress and the California-based chip maker. Nvidia is also facing a shareholder class-action lawsuit.
The alleged Chinese intelligence asset, Alice Huang, is a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed, Nvidia’s primary business partner in Southeast Asia. Founded in 2023, Megaspeed has been investigated over whether it helped Chinese companies access its $4.8bn stock of powerful Nvidia chips.
The affair has raised national security concerns in the US, which restricts chip sales to China over concerns that the Chinese military, intelligence agencies or companies could use the advanced technology.
Huang, 63, is Nvidia’s longtime cofounder, president and CEO. A close ally of US President Donald Trump, he has pushed to ease US restrictions on chip exports to China. Trump said earlier this week that Huang told him he had “absolutely no idea” that he was in a relationship with an intelligence operative.
Huang has agreed to testify before Congress, House of Representatives’ Foreign Affairs Committee Chairman Brian Mast told reporters on Thursday. Senate and House Democrats are also launching a probe into the Trump administration’s plans to approve AI chip sales to Chinese companies.
Huang cofounded the Silicon Valley company in 1993 and owns about 3.6 percent of Nvidia’s stock, making him one of the richest people in the world. He is a powerful figure in the AI industry, which has driven record stock market and wider economic gains. The company is set to report quarterly earnings on Tuesday.
Aleksandar Tomic, associate dean for strategy, innovation, and technology at Boston College, told Al Jazeera that the “resignation of Jensen Huang will have an outsized effect compared to a similar event at another large corporation given the relatively flat structure of Nvidia”.
But he said that Nvidia, an AI bellwether, “is in a very strong position” that will enable the company to transition from Huang’s leadership. Tomic called Huang’s resignation a “speed bump” for the company.
“Nvidia is a strong company with a strong product, and this is a sudden event, not a product of corporate struggle,” he said. “It may take some time to figure out the succession process, but Nvidia can handle it as they approach it from the position of strength.”
Nvidia’s board, which hired a law firm to investigate the affair with Alice Huang, met to decide Huang’s fate on Wednesday shortly after The New York Times reported that Huang also had an extramarital relationship with an Nvidia vice president who reports to him.
On Thursday, the Reuters news agency reported, citing two people familiar with the matter, that Huang also had an intimate relationship with a second company employee.
Nvidia’s stock has tumbled since the scandal erupted, leaving it trading at its lowest multiple of expected earnings in 14 years, Reuters reported.

24,2026-11-12,"The Nvidia leadership shakeup has become its own betting market.
Ever since news broke that Jensen Huang had a relationship with a woman suspected of being a Chinese spy, his future as CEO of Nvidia was in question. On Thursday, Huang announced he'd be stepping down, leaving investors to wonder who would be the next to guide the AI hardware giant. CFO Colette Kress was quickly named interim CEO. According to prediction markets, though, she isn't likely to be the permanent pick. Multiple platforms give longtime hardware executive Ian Buck the highest odds of being the next top executive, at about 35.5%. (Read Business Insider's profile of Ian Buck here.)
""NVIDIA's succession remains far from settled,"" said Ben Mendelowitz, a prediction markets analyst at betting and prediction markets analysis platform, The Action Network. ""The odds highlight the uncertainty between technical expertise and leadership continuity.""
Kress trails Buck by 10 points, with odds of 25.5%. Behind them are two leading external candidates, Arm Holdings CEO René Haas and AMD CEO Lisa Su.
Former Apple CEO Tim Cook, who President Donald Trump touted as the best candidate to replace Huang, has longshot odds of about 6% on Polymarket.
For now, the money is trending toward Buck, a veteran engineer known for his role in developing the Nvidia Compute Unified Device Architecture (CUDA), a parallel computing platform used for GPU applications.
Kalshi currently gives Buck 35% odds and Polymarket's are even higher, at 38% with Kress at 15%.
Mendelowitz added that in the days leading up to Huang's resignation, bettors rushed to wagers on when it might happen, with the odds of his exit surging from 13.2% to 76.6% in just three days as news continued to break about his affairs.
""This dramatic shift highlights how quickly market expectations can change, and a similar swing in the succession odds could emerge as new developments unfold,"" he added.

25,2026-11-12,"Jensen Huang has resigned as the CEO of Nvidia following recent reports of extramarital affairs, including one involving a woman suspected of spying for China. Here are the details.
In late October, The New York Times reported on Jensen Huang’s “close relationship” with Alice Huang, a former managing director at Singapore-based Megaspeed, whom US intelligence officials reportedly identified as a Chinese intelligence asset suspected of passing information to Beijing.
According to subsequent reports, Chinese intelligence allegedly recruited her to gather information about Jensen Huang and influence his position on selling advanced AI chips to Chinese companies. China disputes the espionage allegations, dismissing them as groundless speculation intended to smear the country.
That said, while Nvidia previously denied any impropriety in their relationship, Huang admitted last weekend to having an extramarital affair in a message to employees, saying, “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did.” He did not mention Alice Huang directly.
In the following days, concerns grew over the potential national security implications of the relationship, in addition to Nvidia seeing its stock plummet.
And while Huang remained CEO as Nvidia’s board commissioned an independent investigation into the allegations, The New York Times reported on Wednesday that he had a second extramarital affair, this time with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia’s vice president of healthcare, who reports directly to him.
In the hours that followed, The Wall Street Journal reported that Nvidia’s board was preparing to meet to decide Huang’s future. Soon after, Semafor reported that some board members favored Nvidia CFO Colette Kress as interim CEO, while other reports suggested that Huang’s daughter, Madison Huang, could eventually succeed him. Madison Huang works at Nvidia as senior director of product marketing.
Semafor also named Apple’s chief hardware officer, Johny Srouji, as a potential successor, while Bloomberg reported that Nvidia had hired an outside firm to identify candidates.
Now, Nvidia has officially announced that Huang “has resigned as President, Chief Executive Officer, and a member of the Board, effective immediately.”
The company confirmed that Kress has been appointed interim CEO, while retaining her current responsibilities, until a permanent successor is named. The company is considering both internal and external candidates.
Nvidia also announced that Harvey C. Jones, a longtime technology executive and venture investor, has been appointed the company’s first-ever board chair.
In a statement, Jones said the board has “full confidence in Colette and the leadership team,” adding that Nvidia expects its operations, customer commitments, and product roadmap to continue without interruption.
The shareholder lawsuits over the broad-spectrum mismanagement are going to be incredible. 
No succession plan. Lying to investors to cover up the CEO’s unethical and foolish behavior. Having a super-weak board of directors absent anyone with high-level relevant experience. 
Wow. 
Nvidia did not directly address the allegations surrounding Huang in its announcement, instead thanking him for his contributions to the company over the past 33 years.
Following the Times’ Wednesday report, Nvidia shares plunged in after-hours trading, erasing the day’s 2.9% gain after two consecutive sessions of losses.
The stock continued to fall following today’s announcement, dropping more than 10% at the time of writing from Wednesday’s closing price of $173.73, bringing its total decline to approximately 35% from its October 6 record close.
How Nvidia’s stock will behave when the market opens today and in the days that follow is anyone’s guess, especially with the company scheduled to report its quarterly earnings next Tuesday, November 17.

30,2026-11-12,"I was raised in New Jersey, but moved to the Netherlands in October 1997 to start a new life with the man who would eventually become my husband.
I was 19 and, aside from my family and education, there wasn't much tying me down to the US. I was young, free, and fearless.
Reality hit hard during those first months, though: Even the rosy glow of love couldn't have prepared me for the culture shock and loneliness. I wondered how I would ever learn the language, get an education, find a job, and feel at home.
Undeterred, I immediately began learning Dutch. By the following May, I was studying journalism at a local university. Over the next five years, I married my partner, had a daughter, and enrolled in a graduate program, where I earned a degree in Dutch Studies.
By 2008, I had become a Dutch citizen, launched a writing career, and bought a beautiful four-bedroom home with a glorious garden and dreamy kitchen in Almere, a city in the heart of the Netherlands. Three years later, I landed a dream remote job as a magazine editor.
I was settled, happy, and doing well. And then, I started thinking about leaving.
I still remember how I felt the first time I visited Duras, a village in southwest France surrounded by vineyards, prune orchards, and sunflower fields.
It was 2009, and I was staying with my husband and 9-year-old daughter at a little cottage a stone's throw from the town center. I remember laying eyes on the Château de Duras — a majestic castle that has stood in the village since the 12th century — and feeling an immediate connection that I couldn't quite explain.
In the years that followed, we spent at least two weeks there every summer, and each time we drove away, it felt like I was leaving a little piece of my heart behind.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
My husband was just as captivated, too. Before long, we started asking ourselves the obvious question: What about actually living there?
By 2022, we'd started genuinely considering a move, but it felt daunting. Could I really start all over again, now, in my late 40s?
What about selling the home I had poured so much love into? And what about my daughter? At 23, she had been living independently for some time, but the thought of moving to another country was difficult to contemplate.
Still, I continued scrolling through French property listings — and one day, I spotted a house for sale in Duras. It was a stately maison de maître, with white shutters, stone floors, and even a wine cellar.
Two days later, we went on a whirlwind trip to France, hoping to put in an offer.
From the moment I walked in, I felt the same sense of connection that I did when I first set foot in Duras. I was so smitten that I barely registered how much work the house needed.
After our visit, my husband and I realized that making it habitable would cost at least twice the purchase price, not to mention the time and patience renovations would demand.
Disillusioned, we returned to the Netherlands, only to revisit our plans year after year. We viewed other properties in France, but every time, fear crept in, and we changed our minds at the last minute.
In the spring of this year, we decided to put our house on the market. We knew that if we kept coming up with excuses or waiting for the perfect moment, we might never make the move. Within seven days, it was sold.
As luck (or fate) would have it, we noticed a house for sale in Duras, right next door to the place we had loved so much in 2022. It had the same classic French beauty and character, and it was in excellent condition.
What really caught my attention was the kitchen: it reminded me so much of the one in our Dutch home, which I had found so difficult to leave behind.
We made an offer, and in September, we waved goodbye to our Dutch life and moved to France.
The transition so far hasn't been easy. Nearly three decades in the Netherlands shaped me profoundly, and I miss the ease of city life.
Here in southwest France, life moves at a slower pace, which I'm still getting used to. Even though I have a basic level of French, speaking confidently and with ease is still a challenge.
But still, I wake up every morning and pinch myself. And then I do it again when we drive through the vineyard-covered hillsides, wander past the château, or open a bottle of Duras wine to enjoy with a lunch I've prepared using produce from the Monday market.
Moving to the Netherlands all those years ago taught me that I was stronger than I thought, and that wonderful things can happen when we step outside our comfort zone and embrace the unfamiliar.
Now, I'm realizing that it's never too late to start all over again in pursuit of a long-held dream.

You may well remember that when the Switch 2 launched in June 2025, the version bundled with a download code forÂ Mario Kart World was then priced at $500. But in the 18 months since, the price of the Switch 2 has jumped up $50 thanks to the AI-driven component crisis and Trump’s inane tariffs. Thus, returning to the baseline represents something of a bargain in our upside-down times. That bundled deal, which also comes with three months of Nintendo Switch Online, will run from November 22 to December 5, and obviously depends on available stock.
For a slightly shorter window, a handful of Nintendo’s own games will also get the Black Friday treatment via retailers like Amazon, Best Buy, GameStop and Walmart, as well as Nintendo’s own U.S. stores. From November 22 to 29, Switch 2 titles Donkey Kong Bananza,Â Metroid Prime 4: Beyond, Kirby Air Riders, andÂ Super Mario Party Jamboree will all be $20 less, meaning the very splendid Donkey Kong game will be just $50 instead of $70. Meanwhile the Switch versions ofÂ Super Mario Party Jamboree andÂ Metroid Prime 4Â will also receive twenty-buck reductions.
Running over yet another set of dates (Nintendo never wants anything to be straightforward), the eShop will be having its own Black Friday sale, this time from November 19 to December 2.
