Cars
Is it a bird? Is it a plane? Is it a... flying Cybertruck? Nope, it's a modular flying machine that will take to the skies next year.
Principal Writer
Principal Writer

				Edited by Jon Skillings 
November 12, 2026, 5:23 pm ET
When I first laid eyes on the Aridge flying car at the IFA consumer electronics show in Berlin this past September, I didn’t fully understand what I was looking at.
Taking pride of place on the stand belonging to Chinese robotics company Xpeng, there was a van that bore more than a passing resemblance to a Cybertruck. It didn’t have any wings, and it looked far too dense to fly. Sitting next to it was a kind of oversized drone with a cabin just about large enough to fit two people. 
The two vehicles seemed like totally separate entities. It never would’ve occurred to me to assume they were two halves of one system – much less that the drone, with its substantial wingspan, could fold up to fit inside the van.
More from CNET
I now understand that this modular two-part flying car system is known formally as the Aridge Land Aircraft Carrier. The ground vehicle, also known as the mothership, is a chunky 5.5-meter-long van that can be driven with a standard license and fits in most parking spaces. It’s designed to store, carry and deploy the electric vertical takeoff and landing vehicle, or eVTOL, including its six foldable rotors, which can fly as high as a helicopter for 30km (about 19 miles).
This remarkable creation is the work of Deli Zhao, founder and president of Aridge, which is backed by robotics and auto company Xpeng. Speaking at Web Summit in Lisbon on Tuesday, Zhao presented himself as a man who was inspired to pursue a career building flying cars by Harry Potter and the Green Goblin, despite his lack of formal education.
“Dreams are very important,” Zhao said, as a video of him flying the eVTOL played on a screen in the background. Anyone can learn to fly the vehicle with just five minutes of training, he added. Or if you’re too tired to use the simple joystick, the eVTOL can fly itself autonomously.
“Everyone here can be a pilot,” Zhao said. “Everyone can be a captain.”
This sounds like a story from the very kinds of science fiction that sparked the creation of the Land Aircraft Carrier in the first place – and yet, it isn’t. It’s far from the first time I’ve seen a flying car concept at a tech event. They’ve been showing up for almost a decade at this point. But what marks this one apart is that Xpeng says it has already taken 17,000 orders for the vehicle across China, the UAE and other Asian markets.
“Our advantage is we’re closer to commercialization,” Zhao said. Xpeng has established the world’s first mass-production factory for flying cars in Guangzhou, with trial production beginning in November last year. Right now, it’s ramping up capacity and improving quality with the aim of eventually being able to build a flying car in 30 minutes.
Aridge has worked through many different concepts, including flying motorcycles, to finally find one ready for commercial production (plus it has a six-seater air taxi and integrated flying car in the works for the future too). If you have $300,000 going spare, the Land Aircraft Carrier could soon be yours.
The vast majority of preorders are from individuals, according to Xpeng, but the vehicle has also been designed for use in tourism and search and rescue. Zhao said it solves multiple problems with existing eVTOLs simultaneously: storage (it lives in the back of the mothership), recharging (it charges as the mothership is driving), transportation to and from appropriate flight sites, and ease of use.
“I hope to make flying accessible to everyone as soon as possible,” Zhao said. Right now, it takes around 20 to 30 hours of training to acquire the sport pilot license you’d need to fly the eVTOL. In China, Zhao said he’s in conversation with the regulator to reduce this, emphasizing his vehicle’s ease of use.
Zhao said he’s learned a lot from the aviation sector in China, where the Land Aircraft Carrier has gone through extensive safety testing and will likely take to the skies first as a consumer-owned product as soon as next summer. Growing its low-altitude economy, which encompasses drone delivery and eVTOLs, is national policy for the country – in spite of calls for more regulation after a deadly crash in Beijing this summer.
Xpeng is also eyeing the European market, although the regulatory hurdles are likely to be substantial. “Europe’s aviation sector is highly developed,” Zhao said, expressing his respect for the region’s strict rules, but adding that the market was important. The company will also start exploring certification in the US next year, he said. Actual progress will depend on the outcomes, he added.
In spite of Zhao’s boundless optimism, it’s difficult to buy into the hype around his flying car without witnessing it in action in person. I’ve now seen the Land Aircraft Carrier twice in different locations and videos of it in operation, but I want to observe the eVTOL’s deployment from the mothership and its airborne skills for myself.
At one point during his Web Summit talk, Zhao climbed into the cockpit of the eVTOL on stage. For a moment it seemed as though he might fly it right then and there inside Lisbon’s cavernous ME Arena. The audience raised their phones in unison, only to drop them again in disappointment when they realized Zhao wasn’t actually going to take off.
No matter how idiot-proof it might be to fly, it turns out European health and safety rules still apply.
Disclosure: Katie Collins traveled to Lisbon as a guest of Web Summit to serve as a panel moderator. Her reporting from the event was independent of that role.

36,2026-11-12,"

      John Ruwitch
    


                Jensen Huang gives a keynote address at the Nvidia Berlin GPU Technology Conference on Oct. 21, 2026.
                
                    
                    Sean Gallup/Getty Images

Sign up for alerts on breaking news and exclusive reporting from NPR
Jensen Huang, CEO of the chipmaking juggernaut Nvidia, is stepping down, the company's board announced Thursday.
The decision comes days after the company took the extraordinary step of launching an investigation following news reports alleging that Huang had had an extramarital affair with one of the company's clients, who U.S. intelligence officials concluded had been recruited by China's top spy agency, according to the New York Times. NPR has not independently confirmed those reports.
In an email to Nvidia staff on Sunday, Huang admitted to cheating on his wife, and said he would cooperate with the investigation.
The company's share price dropped sharply on Wednesday, after the New York Times reported that Huang had had a second affair, this one with an Nvidia executive who reported to him.
Nvidia did not immediately respond to an NPR request for comment. 
Huang was widely seen as one of the most important business leaders in the United States. Nvidia dominates the market for the chips used to train and run artificial intelligence. Huang also had President Trump's ear on AI policy, with Trump even calling Huang's phone in September while Huang was onstage during a conference.
The scandal that brought down Huang snowballed quickly over the past two weeks. The New York Times reported in late October that Huang had a close relationship with a Chinese woman at a Singapore-based company that was buying Nvidia chips. Citing unidentified sources, it said U.S. intelligence officials believed she had been recruited by the Chinese government to spy on him and possibly try to influence policy. NPR has not independently confirmed this reporting.
A drip-drip of scoops followed — from the Times and other outlets — suggesting that the relationship was romantic, that senior Trump administration officials knew about the allegations some time ago, and that Huang had had a second affair.
China's foreign ministry has said the allegations of spying are baseless and part of a smear campaign against China.
Huang's departure launches Nvidia into uncharted territory.
Huang's relationship with the alleged Chinese agent and what the Trump administration knew about it will likely be the subject of an investigation when Democrats take control of Congress in January.
""We should all be extremely concerned about this, and the fact that the White House knew about this for weeks, maybe months now, is is is very disturbing as well,"" said Rep. Yvette Clarke, D-NY, a senior member of the House Energy and Commerce Committee in an interview with NPR.
The company's Chief Financial Officer, Colette Kress, has been appointed interim CEO. The board said in their statement they would conduct a full search for a new CEO and consider both internal and external candidates. 
""There's no obvious replacement,"" said Stephen Witt, author of a book about Nvidia called The Thinking Machine. ""It's not even clear Nvidia can be the same kind of company without Jensen. I have a hard time imagining what Nvidia looks like without Jensen.""
Nvidia still plans to report its quarterly earnings on Tuesday. 
Huang was one of the longest-serving CEOs in U.S. corporate history, stepping into the role after launching the company with partners over bottomless cups of coffee at a Denny's restaurant in San Jose, Calif., 33 years ago. Nvidia got its start making specialized chips to create 3D graphics for computers that would come to be known as graphics processing units, or GPUs.
He was the face of Nvidia and an unlikely celebrity, giving countless keynote speeches and interviews in his trademark leather jacket, or posing for selfies with adoring fans.
Huang was also the company's driving force. Under his leadership, Nvidia made a big and early bet on artificial intelligence, investing heavily to transform its niche graphics chips into general-purpose processing units that were ideal for the volume of computations required to create AI.
That bet would pay off in spades as GPUs became the go-to chip for AI creators. When AI burst into the public consciousness in late 2022 with the launch of OpenAI's ChatGPT — a chatbot trained and run on Nvidia chips — the firm was worth about $400 billion. Today, it's valued at more than $4 trillion, among the most valuable in the world, and it dominates the market for GPUs.
But analysts say despite Huang's visionary management of the business, he had a blind spot for succession and hadn't articulated a clear plan, at least publicly.
In a Bloomberg interview from mid-October, Huang was vague about succession plans. ""I'm really hoping that the next CEO of the company is already at the company, but 20 years from now, I hope I stay healthy and I hope I continue to earn my keep.""
Huang's adult children, Spencer and Madison Huang, both work at Nvidia. Asked about talk that Madison might be in line to succeed him, he paused, and told Bloomberg: ""if that's their aspiration, Spencer or Madison's, they have to go earn it. And it's really, really hard work. It takes a lot of sacrifice.""
Daniel Newman, CEO of the Futurum Group, a tech research firm, said the company will inevitably be different without Huang, and its future success is unclear.
""They've always had Jensen as this sort of visionary at the front,"" he said. ""I don't think that's replaceable.""
Katie McQue contributed to this story.

37,2026-11-12,"Instacart is getting into delivering last-minute cocktail ingredients and supplies to bars and restaurants.
The delivery app has struck a partnership with business-to-business alcohol marketplace Provi, the companies confirmed to Business Insider on Thursday. Instacart will offer same-day delivery of items, including mixers and paper products from grocery stores when ordered through Provi's website and app.
The partnership won't include alcohol delivery, the companies said. In the US, laws require alcohol brands to send their products to restaurants and retailers through distributors as part of a three-tiered system.
Instead, Instacart and Provi will work together on ""rapid delivery as fast as possible"" on items that restaurants and bars need in a pinch, such as extra limes for margaritas and napkins for special events, Provi CEO Matthew Tucker said. Many also receive weekly deliveries from more established restaurant distributors.
""What we are trying to solve for is that last-minute out-of-stock,"" Tucker told Business Insider.
The partnership expands Instacart's business-to-business offerings. The delivery service got its start by bringing groceries to individual shoppers' doorsteps but has offered business delivery through a separate division since 2023. Its customers include the Four Seasons resort chain and Van Leeuwen Ice Cream.
Working with Provi allows Instacart to reach bars and restaurants ""where they are shopping today,"" said Andrew Nodes, vice president and general manager of Instacart Business and Supply Chain.
""Now, when a bar or restaurant runs out of herbs or straws mid-service, they have a same-day solution that helps them operate smoothly without missing a beat,"" Nodes said.
It's the first time that Provi is selling non-alcoholic items, Tucker said. The partnership is also coming online at a busy time: the weeks leading up to Thanksgiving and Christmas are when Provi's website and app see the most customer engagement of the year, according to the company.

                                  Add BI in Google so our reporting is easier to find when you’re searching for what matters.
                                
Provi users will see an option to order those items on the platform and will be redirected to Instacart to complete the purchase, he said.
While small, many of the products that Provi and Instacart plan to sell are key to driving sales and avoiding customer disappointment, Tucker added. Drinks tend to be the most profitable items at many bars and restaurants.
""If you don't have the garnishes,"" he said, ""you aren't able to sell some of your highest-margin products.""
Restaurants are also dealing with labor shortages resulting from immigration raids, he said. That means many establishments have fewer staff than ever to make quick grocery runs to keep bars stocked.
""When you have a problem with your labor, you have a problem operating your restaurant,"" Tucker said.

Former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has been summoned to testify next month before a Senate committee about his alleged affair with a woman suspected of being a Chinese spy.
Huang abruptly quit on Thursday as head of one of the world's most valuable companies after revelations of alleged extramarital affairs, in a stunning fall from grace that sent shockwaves through Silicon Valley.
Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick, who is under scrutiny over exports of sensitive Nvidia AI chips to China, has also been summoned to appear before the Senate Banking Committee in December.
Nvidia has lost $550bn (Â£415bn) in stock market value in just five days as the scandal dominated headlines.
Senator Tim Scott sent letters on Thursday to Huang and Lutnick requesting their testimony in front of the Senate Banking Committee.
""Recent reports raise serious concerns about our national security and the protection of sensitive US technology,"" the South Carolina Republican posted on X.
""The American people deserve answers,"" he added.
Scott asked Huang to appear voluntarily on 3 December, but also warned the panel could issue a subpoena to compel his testimony.
Three House of Representatives committees - on intelligence and foreign affairs and a panel on China - have also sent a letter to Huang summoning him for a closed-door interview on 1 December.

Sources have told the BBC that Nvidia employees had noticed the pair were unusually close.
Who is Nvidia's interim CEO Colette Kress - and who could take over permanently?
The rise and fall of Jensen Huang - how the tech superstar became mired in scandal
Huang had been widely expected to weather the storm after the first affair became public, even as Nvidia announced it had launched an investigation into whether its CEO had been ""the target of a foreign intelligence effort"".
One current Nvidia staff member told the BBC that the initial reports of his infidelity were more or less brushed off by rank-and-file workers inside the company. 
It wasn't until Huang's alleged affair with the Nvidia executive became public that workers started to feel ""this is crazy"", the person said.
Another Nvidia employee said they had been told not to speak with the press or otherwise comment on the situation in public.
Many staff have earned millions of dollars in compensation through their ability to cash out their Nvidia shares as the stock soared.
Nvidia has said it has ""no reason to believe that either national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised"", but pledged that its independent inquiry would look at all available information.
The company's meteoric rise had been led by Jensen Huang, who became a major booster of the AI industry, which relies on Nvidia for the powerful computer chips it manufactures. 
Initially those chips were designed for computer gaming. 
But as AI technology evolved in the last four years into chatbots and large language models, companies building AI tools suddenly required huge numbers of the Nvidia computer chips that could process large amounts of data.
Nvidia is now hunting a permanent replacement for Huang. Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress was named on Thursday as interim CEO.
Several tech insiders that the BBC spoke with this week noted that Huang's downfall has been a major topic of conversation in San Francisco Bay area circles, an area filled with tech employees, industry executives and billionaires.
Huang has been married to Lori Huang for 40 years. Both of their children are also executives at Nvidia.
Nvidia's stock fall this week is the most significant five-day drop the company has seen since going public 27 years ago.
Its market value rose by more than 470% in the past five years, peaking just a few weeks ago. It is now down by 31% against a backdrop of wider uncertainty in the tech sector.

40,2026-11-12,"Technology firm Ubiquiti may not be a household name like Apple and Samsung, but since its launch in 2003 it's become a major player in the global networking industry. The New York City-based company develops a wide range of networking products under its UniFi brand, including routers, Wi-Fi access points, and network switches designed to serve businesses, IT professionals, networking enthusiasts, and internet service providers. It also builds routers like the AmpliFi Alien for home users.
While Ubiquiti is a publicly listed company, it's mostly controlled by its founder, chairman, and CEO, Robert Pera, a former Apple engineer who owns 93% of the company's 60.5 million or so shares. Yes, that leaves just 7% for all of the other shareholders combined. The setup actually makes Ubiquiti an unusual public company because while its shares trade on the New York Stock Exchange, Pera's 93% ownership gives him decisive control over things like board elections, company strategy, dividends, buybacks, and other shareholder votes. While outside investors can indeed buy and sell shares in Ubiquiti, they don't really have any meaningful influence over the direction of the business. The market calls this a ""controlled company."" Meta is also in this camp, though through Mark Zuckerberg's control of specialized voting shares.
Ubiquiti's UniFi brand includes products such as network routers, Wi-Fi access points, security cameras, and video doorbells. Pera's company designs its popular products like the UniFi Dream Router and the Cloud Gateway Fiber, for example, manufactured by third-party companies mainly located in Vietnam and China. The only way to know where exactly a Ubiquiti device was made in is by checking the device or box, which should have a label naming the country.
Ubiquiti has become a recognized name in the networking industry, with its devices designed to work together within an integrated ecosystem. One of its main attractions is centralized network management that lets users monitor and configure multiple devices through a single interface, and often at a competitive price. It's this design that makes the company's products appealing to the likes of businesses, IT professionals, and network installers that need to manage multiple locations.
The company competes with a number of established brands. Cisco and Hewlett Packard Enterprise's Aruba Networking Services, for example, are both notable forces in enterprise networking. TP-Link's Omada range meanwhile targets small businesses and cost-conscious buyers. 
Ubiquiti is also up against brands like Netgear and Linksys in the consumer and prosumer networking markets. For small and medium-sized businesses, network installers, and those with a bit of technical know-how, Ubiquiti's UniFi gear is generally seen as a compelling option.
The networking device maker started out by creating high-power radios that could broadcast network access to long-range installations, including through wireless internet service providers. It has since grown to become an established international manufacturer. While it now faces intense competition from much larger companies, Ubiquiti's integrated UniFi ecosystem has helped it to carve out a distinctive position in the global networking market."

The competition is stiff for Samsung 

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Picture this: you upgrade your phone, and it feels like a fresh start. You can finally make the most of advanced features and new tech, but some of them are paywalled behind a silly subscription — well, that’s what the future Samsung experience could look like.
Earlier this week, it was leaked that Samsung might experiment with charging for Galaxy AI, meaning that tools such as Bixby Action, Daily Briefing, and Gen Video will be shunned into a subscription plan. It got me thinking: if you spend good money on a device, would these additional services make the experience any better?
This is something that Apple is particularly notorious for, and once you’ve scored a new device, there’s a whole manner of add-ons out there. Of course there are its flagship streaming services, Apple Music and Apple TV+, but Apple One is a handy way to cut the cord on services by bundling them into one subscription plan.
The difference with Apple is that these add-ons are designed as almost entirely separate services even though they still exist within the Apple ecosystem. For example, Apple Music is an Apple brand, but it operates as its own entity like Spotify or any other major music streaming platform. It’s also available to non-Apple users.
As smartphones develop alongside the evolution of AI tools, more phone manufacturers are starting to embed them into the core mobile experience. Just look at Gemini and Pixel Phones.
With that in mind, is it fair to charge for tools that are already built into the device when you take it out of the box? According to Samsung Galaxy users, it’s not.
While some existing features (Now Brief, Browsing Assist, and Samsung Keyboard tools) are expected to remain free for now, not everyone is thrilled about the possibility of having to pay extra for the complete Galaxy AI experience, as one user on Reddit bluntly puts it (see below).
Get the latest news from Android Central, your trusted companion in the world of Android
But despite the integration of AI services in Samsung’s smartphones, it turns out that not everyone knows they even exist, as another Redditor shares: ‘I have a S25 Ultra, and I'm pretty sure I've never used any of the Galaxy AI stuff. Just looked in my settings and didn't even realize there were so many different AI features available’.
The other issue is the value that’s being brought to the table. Because the market is becoming increasingly saturated, there are a plethora of AI services that can assist you with all manner of things, but for existing Galaxy users, the quality just isn’t there with Galaxy AI.
With Google’s own Gemini services taking the reins of the company’s smartphones and home tech devices, the competition is fierce. In response to the Redditor above, another user added, ‘Most of which can be done better directly with Google services anyway. If I want AI I'll just use actual AI services, though I appreciate there's a local option (which dumbs most of these features down). There are a few useful things, but the vast majority of it is a solution in search of a problem.'
Equally, ChatGPT gives Galaxy AI a run for its money, but even though its premium features are also paywalled, the value factor is unmatched, making it even more difficult for Samsung to compete with the big dogs.
‘The only thing of 'value' is the device automation, which requires device integration. Otherwise, people are better off using external frontier models,’ a Redditor concludes.

43,2026-11-12,"Chip giants stock price falls more than 8 percent amid speculation over co-founder’s position.
Save
Share
Chip giant Nvidia’s stock price has tumbled amid reports that its board of directors is meeting to consider the future of CEO Jensen Huang.
Nvidia shares fell 8.6 percent in after-hours trading on Wednesday as questions swirled over Huang’s position at the top of the $4.2 trillion company.
The plunge comes after The New York Times, The Wall Street Journal and the Financial Times reported that Nvidia’s board of directors would discuss Huang’s position over allegations that he engaged in an extramarital relationship with an Nvidia employee, in addition to an affair with a woman accused of working with Chinese intelligence services.
Nvidia, which launched an investigation into Huang on Sunday, did not immediately respond to Al Jazeera’s requests for comment.
Huang, who co-founded Nvidia in 1993, is one of the most visible faces of the artificial intelligence (AI) boom, spearheading the development of high-end graphics processing units used to train and run frontier models.
The leather jacket-sporting tech executive’s position has also placed him at the centre of the geopolitical rivalry between the United States and China, which are locked in a battle for supremacy in AI.
As a confidant of US President Donald Trump, Huang has lobbied for the easing of restrictions on exports of high-end chips to China, putting him at odds with national security hawks in Washington who argue that Beijing must be blocked from access to cutting-edge US tech.
If Nvidia’s board were to oust Huang, 63, it would mark a seismic moment for the chip giant.
Viewed as integral to Nvidia’s meteoric rise to the ranks of the world’s most valuable companies, Huang is known for a centralised leadership style that places him at the centre of the company’s day-to-day operations and strategic decisions.
“Jensen is Nvidia; without him, the company loses its central, animating spirit,” Jay Goldberg, a senior analyst at Seaport Research Partners in San Francisco, told Al Jazeera.
“The organisation is built around him and replacing him will be very painful organisationally, and impossible to do personality-wise.”
It is unclear whether Nvidia has a succession plan, and analysts see few obvious candidates to take Huang’s place.
Dan Ives, senior managing director at Yorkville Ives, said Huang has been the “hearts and lungs” of Nvidia and that investors will closely scrutinise the executive’s possible replacement.
“We believe Nvidia’s value is $300 per share with Jensen as CEO and $225 per share without Jensen as CEO,” Ives said in a note to clients, adding that the company would navigate the turmoil and “remain a core foundational piece of the AI Revolution.”
“Jensen is not replaceable by another CEO or group of interim executives, but Nvidia likely now has to move on without Jensen at the helm, which seemed like a Twilight Zone episode narrative before Sunday afternoon when the news broke,” Ives said.
Nvidia has been the focus of international intrigue over the past week amid reports that Huang had a relationship with a Chinese woman who US intelligence officials determined had been recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security.
US officials have not provided evidence for the claims about the woman, a Chinese tech executive named Alice Huang.
Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based data centre company Megaspeed, has not publicly commented on the allegations.
China’s Ministry of Foreign Affairs has denied the claims, calling them “entirely groundless, speculative and intended to smear and malign China”.
Nvidia’s board of directors said on Sunday it had opened a probe into whether Huang had been targeted in a foreign intelligence operation, while also expressing confidence in the executive.
Huang, who admitted to being “unfaithful” to his wife and apologised to his family, pledged to cooperate fully with the inquiry and said he was committed to protecting Nvidia and US national security.

Grand Theft Auto 6 is the first major console game to release without a physical version. Take-Two CEO Strauss Zelnick doesn’t think it’ll be the last. Physical games will hang around, but not in any mass-market sort of way. He says they’ll become closer to the equivalent of vinyl records in music.
âIt may not be the end, but listen, weâve been talking, you and I, for a long time,â he told The Game Business‘ Christopher Dring in a new interview. âI predicted that digital distribution would be well over 90 percent of the business more than 10 years ago. I donât always get it right, but this one I got right. Will there always be some physical business? Yes, in the same way that thereâs still a vinyl business in recorded music. But itâs going to be a very small part of the business.â
Physical music accounted for just over 10 percent of total music revenue in the first half of 2026, according to estimates by theÂ Recording Industry Association of America (RIAA). That comes out to $581 million, roughly 80 percent of which was earned through sales of vinyl.
According to Circana, physical game sales in the U.S. last year hit a 30-year low of $1.5 billion. That was about 2.5 percent of the $60.7 billion consumers spent on âvideo game hardware, content, and accessoriesâ in 2025. Physical game sales are already a smaller chunk of overall game spending than vinyl is for music, and that’s before PlayStation game discs go away entirely in 2028. Game discs becoming vinyl would actually be a rosier outlook than where things currently seem to be trending.
We still don’t know how Microsoft will approach physical media with the launch of its Project Helix family of next-gen consoles, and Nintendo has confirmed it has no plans to abandon physical games during the Switch 2 era, even if it’s already taken a half-step in that direction with game key cards.
Will GTA 6 ever get a disc version? Vinyl has been a way for music lovers to immortalize their favorite albums. Surely the biggest game launch ever deserves something similar? Zelnick previously shot down rumors that GTA 6 wasn’t coming to disc. He’s now demurred on whether it could get a physical release in the future. That sort of âinformation always comes from our labels,” he told Variety this week, leaving the ball in Rockstar’s court to decide.
GTA 6 is getting a vinyl release for its soundtrack, however. Seem a bit hypocritical? It’s probably less surprising when, as revealed in Take-Two’s latest financials, only 2 percent of its current revenue comes from game discs.

46,2026-11-12,"More Americans applied for unemployed benefits last week, but jobless claims remain at historically low levels, layoffs are rare and most workers enjoy unusual job security
WASHINGTON -- More Americans applied for unemployment benefits last week, but jobless claims remain at historically low levels, layoffs are rare and most workers enjoy unusual job security.
The Labor Department reported Thursday that jobless claims ticked up to 200,000 last week from a revised 191,000 the week before, coming in higher than the 195,000 economists had forecast. The four-week average of claims, which smooths out week-to-week volatility, was unchanged at 195,250.
Applications for jobless benefits are a proxy for layoffs, and economists watch them closely because they can provide signs of where the job market is headed.
Claims have remained at historically low levels in the year to date, never topping 230,000 in a single week. They’ve come in at or below 200,000 for nine straight weeks.
So far this year, employers — companies, government agencies and nonprofits — have been adding an average of 86,000 jobs a month, including a surprisingly strong 165,000 last month. That is modest by historic standards but an improvement on a lackluster 2025 when monthly job creation averaged 9,700 as high interest rates and President Donald Trump’s unpredictable trade policies discouraged hiring.
Consumer spending has remained resilient and a surge in investment in artificial intelligence has lifted economic growth.

12 November 2026


Samsung Huawei Motorola Apple Android iOS

Samsung was the undisputed foldable king in the third quarter of this year, reports Smart Analytics Global (SAG) based on data from its Smartphone 360 research service. Overall, the foldable market shrank 2% year-over-year to 7.5 million units shipped for the three-month period.
Samsung held 69% market share, its highest since 2023. Meanwhile, Huawei lost 4 percentage points, but remains in second place. Lenovo/Motorola gained 2 percentage points as it rolled out the Razr 70 series to new regions. As for other brands, their market share declined significantly.
Samsung’s success is attributed to the Galaxy Z Fold8, which amounted to over half of Samsung’s global shipments for the quarter. It also helped Samsung achieve “double-digit” growth of foldable shipments in Europe and South Korea.
According to Ice Universe, Samsung has shipped approximately 2.85 million Z Fold8 units in the 55 days between its launch on August 7 and the end of the quarter on September 30. That would mean that the wide foldable accounted for 55% of Samsung’s total foldable shipments and works out to over 50,000 units shipped per day.
It must be noted that Q3 is before the iPhone Duo started shipping on October 23, so it’s not on the chart. SAG has revised down its estimate for the full 2026, in part because of Apple’s production woes with the Duo. It’s not just that, though – demand for foldables in China is lower than expected. That will hurt Apple as China is one of its biggest markets.
This is also a major reason for Huawei’s drop – China is the company’s primary market (it does have a limited presence on the global stage, but not like it did before). Here’s something interesting – the odd-looking, non-folding Huawei Pura X View has cannibalized sales of the Pura X and Pura X Max, which kicked off the wide foldable craze.
There’s good news for 2027 – SAG estimates that the global foldable market will grow by 55% year-over-year. And Apple will capture an impressive 40% of the market, once it gets its iPhone Duo production line in order. It, as well as the Galaxy Z Fold8 and other wide foldables, is expected to account for two-thirds of the foldable market next year.

55,2026-11-12,"Storage used to be the part of a build that was simple: you'd pick your type and capacity, look for something that was discounted, and then move on with more important decisions. In 2026, it can be one of the items that makes you want to forget about putting together a PC. AI demand has captured much of the NAND flash that's available, and consumers are left paying incredibly inflated prices. A 4TB SSD that could once be found for $300 or $400 is now close to $1,000.
One corner of the fast storage market has held up a lot better, and that's used enterprise stock. U.2 SSDs specifically are some of the best value you can find for fast storage at the moment, and while getting it to work in a normal PC takes an adapter and a little more planning than a standard drive, it can be well worth the trouble.
The pre-owned enterprise storage market is alive and well
Used enterprise drives, U.2s included, are priced based on pure supply more than they are based on what the new storage contracts say, and that's partially why they're still affordable. Of course, part of the value comes from the fact that they've been used, but a lot of these drives get decommissioned before anything on them is close to failing. 
Enterprise drives are rated for far more writes than consumer drives, often several times as many at the same capacity. A used enterprise drive with plenty of wear on it can still have more rated life left than a brand-new consumer flagship, and cost less. Unfortunately, the prices don't stay completely decoupled from what new drives are going for. The used market does creep up slowly behind what new stuff is being sold at, so buying sooner rather than later might be beneficial if you're trying to save.
You can save money without hurting performance by choosing SATA
Most consumer SSDs write into a fast SLC cache, then slow sharply once it fills. Enterprise drives generally skip SLC caching and are tuned for steady-state performance, so something like a large file transfer or disk migration doesn't suddenly tank in speed halfway through.
Perhaps the biggest difference between an enterprise U.2 SSD and a normal one is the power-loss protection. Virtually all of these enterprise drives will carry capacitors that keep the controller on-board alive just long enough to flush its cache to the flash if power drops. This helps in cases where you're using ZFS or handling a database where a sync-write needs to be acknowledged, because if there were no capacitors and you were forcing sync-writes on a consumer drive, it'll drop the performance significantly. 
Outside of a home lab environment, the usefulness is a bit more narrow but still there. Video editors and anyone else who regularly moves huge files get the same benefit from steady sustained writes, since a long export or a footage dump never hits a cache cliff. For the gamers and everyday desktop users, an enterprise drive won’t feel any faster than a good consumer drive, and the power-loss protection goes largely unused. What it does offer there is room: as a game library or a bulk storage drive, a used U.2 drive buys a lot of fast capacity for the price. I just wouldn't expect it to beat your NVMe consumer drive you boot from.
It might take a bit of work, but these drives are severely underrated
The cheapest route is an M.2-to-U.2 cable, which runs from a motherboard M.2 slot to the drive’s SFF-8639 connector. It does need a separate SATA power lead because U.2 drives run on 12V, so that's another thing to keep in mind if you go that route. You can go the PCIe add-in card route too, and you can run up to four drives off of one x16 slot with the help of bifurcation, if that's supported on your motherboard.
Once connected, the drive is seen as an ordinary NVMe device by your system with no separate drivers needed, and it can boot a UEFI system, though sometimes these drives add noticeably to boot times. After you're in an OS, checking with a tool like nvme-cli and smartctl is imperative, especially if there's a return window specified for your drive.
There are two very real problems running a U.2 drive that go beyond just the requirement for an adapter. The first is heat: putting one of these in an area of your chassis that doesn't get active airflow probably isn't a good idea, as they can easily pull upwards of 10W under load and idle around 3 or 4 watts. Putting them in an area with dead air is asking for throttling.
The second problem is signal integrity, and it's a lot less forgiving as you go up in PCIe generation. Even jumping from PCIe 3.0 to 4.0, passive adapters can be pretty hit-and-miss, and the OS doesn't always give you a clear idea that it's an issue.
Power-loss protection is invaluable for an SSD deployed in your home server
With some decent airflow and forcing the drive to run at PCIe 3.0, both of those issues can be fixed. There's a bit of extra thought that has to go into putting a pre-owned enterprise U.2 in your system, but that's part of what makes them affordable. If the friction wasn't there, everyone would be buying them. They definitely won’t stay this cheap relative to new ones forever, but for storage that runs all day doing real work, nothing at retail matches what a decommissioned enterprise SSD can do.
We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

56,2026-11-12,"SYNTH ANATOMY uses affiliation & partner programs (big red buttons) to finance a part of the activity. If you use these, you support the website. Thanks!    Reading Time:  6 minutes
The surge of AI-generated apps is overwhelming Apple’s app review process, leaving developers caught in the crossfire – two examples among many.
A flood of new iOS apps has emerged over the last few months. An estimated 10–20 new iOS music apps enter the market every day. This is because AI now makes it possible for anyone to vibe code their own apps and AUv3 plugins.
However, getting an app into the Apple App Store requires going through the app review process. AI has affected not only the number of new releases per day but also Apple’s holy app review process, creating more nightmares than joy for many developers.

Developing and releasing a VST plugin is simple. You set up a website, upload your installers, set a price, and you’re good to go. It’s more complicated on iOS.
To publish an app, you must first join the Apple Developer Program, which costs $99 per year. As a member, you can submit apps with AUv3 support, but they won’t become available immediately. 
Whether they are hand-coded or created with the help of AI, all apps must go through Apple’s review process. Every app, whether a synthesizer, effects, game, or utility app, must first get through this barrier.
This process verifies that the app meets requirements, including security standards. That’s a major advantage over many new, freely available VST plugins . You can be confident that everything you download to your iOS or macOS device has been checked and is free of anything suspicious.
So far, the process from submission to release has taken 24 to 48 hours, and sometimes a few days. Developers say the typical review takes a day or two. Apple’s own App Review guidelines state:
App Review works around the clock, typically reviewing at least 50% of submissions in less than 24 hours and 90% in less than 48 hours.”

24-48 days or a few days! That’s what most developers are currently dreaming of.  The Apple App Review process is currently on the verge of collapsing due to the flood of new iOS apps (AI or not), or even hallucinating, if you ask iOS developers.
Various developers, including AudioThing, Igor Vasiliev, Sugar Bytes, and others, say their new apps have been stuck in App Review for weeks rather than days, with no updates. As a result, many releases focused on cross-platform compatibility are currently struggling.
Take Pittsburgh Modular, for example: their new Microvolt 3901 synth was released as a VST on October 27th, while the iOS version only arrived today, November 12th. However, they planned a simultaneous release.
Igor Vasiliev‘s new HollowTone app (go check out that brilliant app!) also spent weeks stuck in the review process.  Most developers can handle a few delays, provided the app is good and ultimately gets purchased.
The problem arises when the app review process becomes erratic—or stops accepting the app altogether—even though it’s just another synth. Some developers have reported this.

Unfiltered Audio—which offers a range of high-quality AUv3 plugins in the App Store, including the brilliant Battalion drum machine—had trouble bringing its Sinusoidal Run Rhythm to market.
Michael Hetrick of Unfiltered Audio, who’s been in the iOS game for years, wrote on the Loopy Pro forum:
Apple seems to be using AI for automated App Store reviews. SRR got rejected 4 times. The first time, they asked for a video of me demonstrating it (which almost always happens with our plugins).
The second time, the editor hallucinated that “Polyharmonic” was an audio technology that we needed to show a documented license for to use the term in the description. The third time, it asked for a video again, this time with the app connected to the “required proprietary hardware”. The fourth time, it flagged SRR MIDI as spam because of its similarity to SRR.
It was over a month of back-and-forth, so I threw my hands up and released it audio-only for now. What we will do is bundle the MIDI build into the binary so both AUv3s install.
The app is now available, but only in the audio version. The MIDI version is still not out. 
AudioThing Materia took 25 days to get approved (1 rejection), and the latest Seconds, in collaboration with Hainbach, has been in review for 29 days and counting.
The second case shows that this new Apple app review process AI reality can also cost app developers their livelihoods—especially those who have spent months coding their apps.
Marek Dendes is a new developer who is currently caught in this precarious situation. He has been working for several months on a flagship Synthesizer called CRUX Synth—a sort of “Serum 2” for iOS.
I’ve been testing the beta for a few weeks now, and it truly is a flagship product. At the moment, it seems the synthesizer won’t be released, as the app has been rejected multiple times by Apple’s App Review process.

He wrote in the Loopy Pro forum:
My app was REJECTED from submission FOUR TIMES in row. Always same reason (eg. my app is “design spam” and “duplicates existing apps” and it is createed using “app generation template services”) as shown bellow in screenshots. Which is obvious nonsense.
I replied on every rejection – no change, again rejected. Obviously nobody reads my explanations. Thing is it goes from “Waiting for Review” into “In Review” and then instantly in second to “Rejected” so this is some automated process and nobody even reads anything i write – so it’s a bit dead end.
I submitted multiple appeals to the App Review Board – that did not help, did not get any reply (it’s about 5 days since first appeal submit). Tried also to submit only CRUX without effects to avoid multiple apps submitted at once – same result. There is nothing more i can do.
I reached out to my contacts at Apple, but I didn’t get any real help from them regarding this case.
iOS/iPadOS is a fantastic platform for making music on the go. It’s been part of my musical toolkit from the start, and for more than six years, it’s also played an integral role in my hybrid live performance setup.
If you need to submit an app now for release before year’s end, that’s genuinely problematic: you could wait three weeks only to have it rejected. I’m in daily contact with iOS developers.
Many have lost their former euphoria; instead, they express disillusionment and doubt that things will ever be the same. Developers are frustrated by lengthy release delays—and remember, not all submissions are music apps.  

With so many new apps launching, even great ones get lost. Do you remember what was published two days ago? This reflects a problem Apple created years ago: ending its affiliate program left media outlets unable to cover iOS meaningfully.
That’s why I’m on Patreon. Today, written coverage of iOS music apps is rare unless they’re backed by major names like Roland, Korg, or Moog. 
The iOS market is currently off-kilter—not just because of the flood of AI apps, but also because of Apple itself.  Where is the real Apple Intelligence when you actually need it?
If they don’t get a handle on this and start reaching out to iOS developers in person again, as they did early on, I can see the market entering a strange period.
Hey Claude, take me back to the App Store era of 2022. Right now!
More information here: Apple

But there are other ways to make money and you could argue that not having an affiliate program can make reviews and coverage more impartial. After all, why would you be critical of an app you could make a commission from if someone buys it via your link?
1) No, you can’t release plugins independently for the iOS/iPadOS platform. You have to go through this process. macOS is different because you have VST, VST3, AUv2, CLAP and AAX plugins. 
2) I use affiliate links, and I critique apps and plugins whenever there is something to find wrong, whether I have a link or not. You are an affiliate partner of the platform, not of the developer directly. 
More importantly, it has motivated more media outlets to talk about them and keep them in the spotlight—which is especially significant for tiny indie developers who lack the massive budgets needed for advertising. This kind of coverage—whether positive or negative—has been missing ever since. There used to be media outlets that focused exclusively on music apps, but they’re all gone now because the activity was no longer financially viable.

The irony is a lot of this is money-driven.
I’m not going to bother commenting here any more, you seem very defensive about a lot of stuff. Good luck Tom.
(Oh and you might want to upgrade your hosting if money isn’t too much of a big issue for you, as there are many gateway and other errors)
When a new app shows up from an unknown developer, I look for three specific thing before I jump in shear first… a website with a robust description of the app, links to YouTube videos and a list of ways to contact them.
A lot of the recent app spam has very little of any of those things. I’m not saying that a lot of those things are ai generated app releases, but… there sure is a lot of laziness involved in the cases where they can’t even bother to provide a support link.
Strange, for me I had an approval in 24 hours after one rejection which made me add a screen recording. Unfortunately, I found a blocker bug and now that I’ve fixed it, it’s been about 3 days it’s stuck in Waiting For Approval. Maybe I was lucky with my timing on the first submission because it was in the middle of the weekend?
It’s funny how much AI is trying to combat the problems of AI and then creating new problems…
I get why Apple wants automated triage, but using AI to reject without a human appeal path is just shifting the cost onto indie devs who can’t afford weeks of delay.

Thanks for writing this article, Tom. 
As an iOS music app developer for over 10 years (and music software for 30+ years) I can only mirror what you’ve written. It’s a crazy mess out there. Any decent release gets instantly buried under a dozen vibe coded clone apps, and the user community has “new app fatigue” because wading through all the Claude-generated hype promotion material gets really tiresome.
The AI slopocalypse is a scourge for devs and users alike. My motivation and enjoyment of building creative music tools has certainly taken a major hit lately.
Honestly, I can’t imagine a better time to be alive than right now. More people being able to provide the community with creative tools is really awesome! You call it AI slop, I call it an opportunity for everybody to express themselves in creative tools and share their excitement with everybody.
I also disagree with the sentiment that this new wave of apps is the downfall of established developers. Why would it be? They surely will be able to continue to provide killer apps with at least the same high quality as before. It’s not like musicians will suddenly stop buying apps from established devs. Competition stimulates the market.

Jensen Huang co-founded Nvidia over 30 years ago and has has an estimated net worth of $145bn (Â£109bn)
Tech billionaire Jensen Huang has indicated he is willing to appear before Congress to discuss whether US national security was compromised, following a major scandal at one of the most valuable companies in the world.
Huang resigned as the CEO of Nvidia, the enormously successful AI chip company, after it was reported he had two affairs: one with suspected Chinese spy Alice Huang and another with senior employee Kimberly Powell.
The company's stocks plummeted as US markets opened shortly after Huang's resignation, and while it recovered some ground, it closed more than 4% down, with the firm shedding more than half a trillion in market value.
Huang has been replaced for now by the company's Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress who will look to steady the ship at the company.
Dubbed the ""Taylor Swift of tech"", Huang transformed Nvidia from a video game chip business to develop semiconductors critical for developing AI.
Access to advanced computer chips has become a key battleground between Beijing and Washington, writes our China correspondent. Nvidia's powerful B200 and B300 chips cannot be legally exported to China. Beijing has called allegations of spying a ""smear"".
We are now ending our live coverage. You can read more about the story here.
Nvidia makes chips used in artificial intelligence (AI)
systems. 
It has cemented a
reputation as one of the world's premier chip designers, and is one of the most valuable companies in the world. 
A computer chip is essentially a small piece of semiconductor material, or a set of electronic circuits. It processes information and stores data. 
Nvidia is far from the only company that designs computer chips, which more broadly are used in all modern digital technology, from computers to cars to kitchen appliances. But Nvidia chips are used for many cutting-edge technologies, and are widely used in the AI industry.
The company was founded in 1993. Before the AI boom took off, it was largely known for making the type of chips that process graphics, particularly for computer games.
It has widely been considered a stock market darling in recent years, with the AI boom driving a meteoric rise in its share price.
Yesterday - even after allegations that Huang had had affairs caused it to tumble, and with concerns about further AI regulation after the Democrats won Congress - the stock was trading at around $170. That's up from around $30 five years ago - an increase of around 470%.
Trump presented Huang with a science award in October
US President Donald Trump briefly referred to Jensen Huang's resignation  when he called into an interview earlier today. 
Speaking on The Hugh Hewitt Show, Trump says that the US would ""be in bad shape"" if it lost its edge in the AI race with China, again referring to the technology as super intelligence (SI).
He also mentions how last month he honoured the Nvidia co-founder with the National Medal of Science during a summit in Washington - then weighs in on Huang's predicament.
""What a horrible thing that is. Thatâs so sad to see that happen,"" he says.
When asked about suspected Chinese espionage relating to AI, Trump says his oft-repeated line that the US also spies on China.
""We also go after them. You know, itâs not just a one-sided deal. Somebody said 'oh, theyâre tapping our phones.' I think thatâs right. Weâre tapping their phones too. And we are, and they are too. And itâs been that way for a long time,"" he says.
China has rejected spying allegations relating to Huang.

Chris Coons, also a Democratic senator, says on X that Huang ""has spent years lobbying Trump to allow advanced AI chip sales to China"". As of now, there are ""serious questions"" about his ties to an alleged Chinese spy and what Trump knew, he says, adding ""Congress needs answers"".
Republican Chairman of House Select Committee on China, John Moolenaar, describes Huang's departure as ""an unfortunate end that could have been avoided"".
""In Congress we must act quickly to pass bipartisan export control legislation in the year-end national defense bill,"" he says.
Responding to reports that Huang had an affair with an alleged Chinese spy, Nvidia previously said it had ""no reason to believe"" the company's secrets or national security were compromised, while Huang said he was committed to ""supporting America's national security"".
China publicly rejects the spying allegations as a smear, and the Foreign Ministry in Beijing warned Washington the allegations should not become a reason for it to again impose restrictions on technological sales.
Bernd Debusmann JrWhite House reporter in Washington DC
Jensen Huang's resignation from Nvidia is not the end of the scandal, which will continue to reverberate around Washington for days, weeks and possibly months to come. 
Going forward, much of the focus will be on the Trump administration's Commerce Secretary, former Cantor Fitzgerald president Howard Lutnick. 
According to the New York Times, Lutnick clashed with Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent over his handling of Megaspeed, the Singaporean data centre firm that was led, in part, by the woman alleged to be a Chinese spy with whom Huang reportedly had an affair. 
While the commerce department has denied the clash took place, the report starkly highlights tensions within the Trump administration over its AI policy. A particular focus is technology export rules, which are overseen by the commerce department, and the over $4bn of Nvidia technology purchased by Megaspeed.
Even before today's resignation, House Democrats had publicly demanded that Lutnick be called to answer questions about the administration's AI and export control policies, which they have claimed endanger US national security. 
It has also been reported by Axios was sent a highly classified memo regarding Huang's affair and the alleged connection to Chinese intelligence. 
Lutnick has so far not commented on the issue, and Trump has suggested only that Apple's Tim Cook take the reins of Nvidia.
US Attorney General Todd Blanche
US Attorney General Todd Blanche says the Department of Justice has not yet drawn any conclusions about whether or not Alice Huang, the woman Jensen Huang is alleged to have had an affair with, is a Chinese spy. 
Speaking to reporters during a press conference on Thursday, Blanche says: ""Of course the DOJ is investigating or looking at"" the allegations that Alice Huang works for the Chinese government. 
Blanche has also been asked about the commerce department's ongoing investigation into the potential illegal smuggling of restricted Nvidia chips to China. He says he cannot comment on the status of the investigation, but says the department regularly seeks counsel from the DOJ in these types of investigations. 
""The DOJ has a role in investigating if there are chips that shouldn't be sold. It could potentially be in violation of our export laws,"" he says, which he adds, could be civil, regulatory or criminal depending on the case.
Beijing has previously warned the US not to use the spying allegations as a pretext to restrict tech sales to China.

Michelle FleuryNew York business correspondent
With Jensen Huang out as Nvidia CEO, it is worth stepping
back and looking at the bigger story. This is about more than one executive or
one company.
The suspected spying scandal raises important questions
about the relationship between the US and China, the limits of export controls
and the growing influence of big technology companies on government policy.
Nvidia has become a central part of that debate. The Trump
administration allowed the company to resume some chip sales to China in return
for a share of the revenue.
Nvidia agreed to pay the US government 15% of sales of its
H20 chips and 25% of sales of its more advanced H200 chips.
The arrangement raises questions about how much influence
Nvidia had over the rules governing its own business, and where the interests
of a private company end and those of the US government begin.
It also shows the risks of putting a company at the centre
of a national strategy. Nvidia is now caught between Washington's security
concerns, Beijing's ambitions and the global race to develop Artificial Intelligence.
The wider lesson is that when governments turn private
companies into national champions, they also expose them to risks that go well
beyond business.
In a statement on Sunday, Nvidia's board said it was
launching an ""independent investigation"" in light of reports that its
chief executive, Jensen Huang, had been ""the target of a foreign
intelligence effort"".
It said it had ""no reason to believe that either
national security or Nvidia secrets were compromised"", but said the

Donald Trump said the FBI was investigating the affair
between Huang and the alleged Chinese spy. The US president told reporters on

was a spy"". Trump said: ""I really believe him.""
US lawmakers on Capitol Hill say Huang has agreed to appear
before Congress for questioning, according

Days after the Nvidia scandal first broke, the Singapore government has given a statement regarding the suspected Chinese spy Alice Huang and Megaspeed, the Singaporean company she was associated with.
The statement says that Huang was never investigated for espionage.
However, she was investigated last year as part of a probe into several companies including Megaspeed, over potential false declarations in relation to the purchase of computer servers.
Authorities said they took her passport in June 2025 to ensure she could not leave Singapore during the investigations.
In February this year, authorities concluded there was insufficient evidence that Huang had committed offences regarding the potential false declarations.
However, they found she had ""provided information which she knew to be inaccurate... to downplay her role in Megaspeed"". 
Authorities gave her a stern warning and banned her from Singapore. They returned her passport on 26 February, and she left the same day.

The BBC has approached the company for further comment on the statement from the Singaporean government.

Tech > Services & Software > AI
A new study dives into the consequences of collaborating with AI on creative endeavors, and what it means for output and satisfaction at work.
Writer
Writer

				Edited by Jon Skillings 
November 12, 2026, 12:17 pm ET
Did you use AI at your job today? Maybe it helped you work faster. But too much AI may reduce both the quality of your work and your satisfaction with the job, according to a study published Nov. 10 in the PNAS Nexus science journal. 
The team behind the study knew from prior research that generative AI was a mixed bag, capable of dramatically boosting productivity, but also prone to throttling creative diversity and worker satisfaction.
“The question we wanted to answer was whether these trade-offs are inevitable,” Kartik Hosanagar, a University of Pennsylvania professor of technology, digital business and marketing, and one of the study’s authors, told CNET via email. “Our research suggests they aren’t.”
More from CNET
AI is changing work quickly, with dizzying contradictions. Tech companies like Microsoft and Meta are spending millions on integrating AI into their business for greater efficiencies and output, while getting rid of human workers through mass buyouts and layoffs. Workers who use AI report some advantages, like saving 40 to 60 minutes a day at work, while suffering consequences like loss of confidence, loss of skills and a tougher labor market. 
Teachers have also sounded the alarm, saying AI is essentially ending homework. Researchers say that if we don’t pay attention to how we collaborate with AI, we could start losing critical thinking along the way. 
“Think about GPS navigation. It’s incredibly useful, but many of us have lost the ability to navigate even familiar neighborhoods without it,” Hosanagar said. “Something similar could happen with AI and cognitive skills.”
The study by Hosanagar’s team focused on the different ways we collaborate with AI on creative tasks. They found that the way we use AI — not just whether or not we use it — has a big effect on the final product and on our perception of the work.
“The future of work isn’t just about how capable AI becomes,” Hosanagar said, “but how we design the collaboration between humans and AI so as to support a broader set of goals.” 
The researchers tasked 285 students, split into three groups, with writing assignments over two days. On the first day, no students used AI for writing. On the second day, each group used a different process for writing with AI. 
Each of the groups used a separate “collaboration design” when working with the AI on the writing assignment. The collaboration designs represent different ways people interact with AI on the job. All three approaches delivered similar productivity benefits, but differed significantly in quality, satisfaction and creative diversity, according to Hosanagar.
In the first group, Human Confirmation, the AI planned, outlined, wrote and edited the assignment, with the humans just approving the work during each step. In the second, Human Creativity, humans did the outlining and planning, while AI did the writing and editing. In the third, Copilot, humans and AI collaborated during the entire process. 
The researchers found that using AI in creative writing did not improve the quality of the work in any of the groups, but it did speed up the task. 
People in the Human Confirmation produced the lowest quality and least diverse work, and participants reported feeling unsatisfied with it. But the other two groups, Human Creativity and Copilot, produced better work at the same speed, with participants reporting a more positive experience. 
The study showed that the best outcomes happen when humans are actively involved in creative tasks, especially generating ideas and creating outlines, while AI assists with execution, Hosanagar said. “The goal shouldn’t be to eliminate human effort, but to direct that effort toward the parts of the process where human judgment and creativity matter most.” 
It would be good to be wary, too, of the unintended consequences of people playing a smaller role in their daily work as they collaborate with AI. 
“Our research also suggests that people derive satisfaction from having a meaningful creative role, not simply from producing a good final output,” Hosanagar said. “So, we should be thoughtful about what we automate.”

Explore the future of retail with AI shopping. Understand how AI affects product visibility and decision-making for consumers.
This post was sponsored by Writesonic. The opinions expressed in this article are the sponsor’s own.
When shoppers ask ChatGPT or Google what to buy, answers often come back as product cards with prices, ratings, and “buy now” links. AI writes those cards from data spread across the web, but few brands know how those cards position their products.
That’s a risky blind spot to have heading into the holidays. Adobe forecasts AI traffic to US retail sites will grow 130% this holiday season.
AI shopping results usually go wrong in three ways. Your product doesn’t show up, it shows up with the wrong details, or a competitor or reseller gets the final click instead of you. The good news is that these errors trace back to product data you control.
This guide covers what these results look like, why they go wrong, and how to audit your own products.
AI shopping results are product recommendations that appear inside an AI answer. They’re usually triggered by a specific buying question, like “waterproof hiking boots for wide feet under $150.”
A single result often includes:
In March 2026, OpenAI expanded its Agentic Commerce Protocol (ACP) to cover product discovery. Merchants now share product feeds through ACP so their catalogs appear in ChatGPT.
Retailers like Target, Best Buy, and Wayfair are already integrated. Shopify merchants flow in through Shopify Catalog.
OpenAI dropped in-chat Instant Checkout in March 2026 and now lets merchants run checkout on their own sites. ChatGPT helps you get the shopper, but your product page still has to close the sale.
AI Overviews now show product cards for many shopping searches, drawing on Google’s Shopping Graph. Merchants add their products mainly by uploading feeds to Google Merchant Center. Google says the Shopping Graph holds over 50 billion product listings.
AI Mode and Gemini use the same Shopping Graph, and Google is rolling out agentic checkout there too. In this scenario, Google’s agent buys a tracked item with Google Pay once the shopper confirms. To qualify, merchants need a Merchant Center account and a Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP) integration.
The most common AI shopping issues fall into three groups.
Most brands find these issues by accident. Someone runs a prompt, sees a wrong price, and fixes that one product. Hundreds of other prompts go unchecked.
The audit takes four steps.
Errors trace back to one of four inputs.
The common thread is consistency. When your feed, product page, and retailer listings disagree on price, stock, or naming, AI has to pick one. It doesn’t always pick right.
Some quick wins before Black Friday:
An analysis reported by Search Engine Journal tracked ChatGPT product picks that came from integrated feeds. In one day in July 2026, their share went from 8.26% to 61.54%. Of 517 tracked stores with visibility swings of 33% or more, 450 saw drops.
Merchant variety shrank too. The top 10 merchants’ share of references grew from 22.5% to 41.8%. Stores without a connected feed now risk losing picks they used to win.
Your product also lives on Amazon, Walmart, Target, and other retailers. Each listing can show a different price, title, or stock status. AI may link to whichever one looks most complete.
Product schema, plus identifiers like Global Trade Item Number (GTIN), manufacturer part number (MPN), brand, and variant, tells AI exactly which item it’s looking at. Without them, AI can mix up models, sizes, and colors.
AI leans hard on what third-party websites say about your brand and products. Writesonic’s June 2026 study of 2,000+ brands found YouTube or Reddit mentions made a brand about three times more likely to show up in AI answers. Mentions on its own site raised that just 1.3 times.
Manual audits break down fast. Each check is only a snapshot, and AI answers change constantly.
Results vary by platform, region, and day, so rerun the same prompts over time. Daily checks give you the most accurate picture. Audit at the category level too, because a brand can dominate headphones and be invisible in speakers. How do you do this? A strong AI Visibility platform will help you collect, understand and action this data.
Writesonic’s study of 631,999 prompt runs across seven AI platforms found the top-ranked brand in ChatGPT rotated on 52% of prompts when the same prompt was rerun.
Writesonic’s AI Shopping Tracker automates those checks daily across ChatGPT and Google AI Overviews. It also shows how often your prompts trigger shopping results at all, which manual audits miss.
It maps directly to the problems covered above:
Any tracker only sees the prompts you give it, so the prompt set from your audit is the foundation. Start with your best sellers and the categories you can’t afford to lose this season.
The brands that check what AI says about their products, and fix the data behind it, will own these product cards. Everyone else is letting a model decide how their products are shown.

By 
January Nelson 


Updated 1 day ago,
November 12, 2026


Nvidia’s board is reportedly weighing Jensen Huang’s removal after the report of a second relationship, this one with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia’s vice president of healthcare, who reports directly to Huang.
The push came hours after that second relationship was reported. Citing five people who worked at the company, the account said the two were recorded being affectionate at a San Jose hotel in March during Nvidia’s annual conference, and that Huang’s security detained the bystander who filmed them and offered a nondisclosure agreement.
Huang, Powell and Nvidia have not confirmed it. When a reporter reached Huang, he reportedly said, “I don’t think you should be talking to me directly,” and hung up.
Dismissal is among the options still open to the board, which is preparing to push Huang out and could meet as early as Wednesday, according to people familiar with the matter. Separate reporting said the board was meeting to decide, and that some Trump administration officials expected he would be ousted.
Nvidia’s expansion into medical imaging, health care and higher education came to have a public representative in Powell. Now 48, she joined the company in 2008.
Huang had already acknowledged a first relationship, though only in general terms, before this second report. In a Sunday, November 8 email to employees, he said he had been unfaithful to Lori, his wife of more than 40 years.
“I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry,” he wrote, without naming the other person.
She has been identified as Alice Huang, no relation, a Chinese citizen and former senior executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore company that bought roughly $4.8 billion of Nvidia chips over about two years. US officials have concluded she was an asset of China’s Ministry of State Security; Beijing denies it and calls the story a smear.
At 63, Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993, has led it ever since, holds about 3.6% of the stock, and has said roughly 60 people report directly to him. Analysts have long seen little prospect of finding a replacement for him. A succession plan has not been made public.
Three days ago the board said it had “confidence in Jensen and his leadership team.”
Wednesday’s closing price was near $174; in after-hours trading, shares reached roughly $160, a drop of about 8%. Counting that decline, about $600 billion had been lost during the week since the board announced its investigation.
After peaking October 6 near $5.8 trillion in market value, the stock has dropped more than 25%, a loss of more than $1.5 trillion, with Apple overtaking it as the world’s most valuable company.

Shares in Nasdaq-listed Bit Digital fell 9.3% in premarket trading on Thursday after the Ethereum treasury and AI infrastructure company reported third-quarter revenue below analysts’ estimates.
Bit Digital reported $27.1 million in revenue, down 11% from $30.5 million a year earlier. Net income attributable to shareholders was $28.5 million, or 8 cents per diluted share, according to its Thursday SEC filing.
The company’s revenue came in nearly 16% below analyst consensus estimates of about $32.2 million, marking a $5.1 million shortfall, according to Yahoo Finance.
Bit Digital reported $1 million in Ether (ETH) staking revenue, down about 66% from $2.9 million a year ago. The company reported holdings equivalent to about 163,726 ETH, worth $439.4 million as of Sept. 30.
Its Bitcoin mining revenue fell to $2 million from $7.4 million a year earlier.
Bit Digital said that the decline in mining revenue was “consistent with the continued winddown of the business.” The company revealed it pivoted its corporate treasury strategy from Bitcoin to Ether in July 2025.

65,2026-11-12,"


          US editor
        

      Photography by         Helio Leite
                
Engineering company Crosslam has designed and built Aurora 275, a hybrid building with mass-timber elements that the company claims is the tallest of its type in Brazil. 
Standing 22 metres tall, Aurora 275 is considered a hybrid-timber building, with a structural system consisting of a reinforced concrete core, metal, and mass-timber elements including glued-laminated timber (glulam) beams and columns.

Located in the Suzano area of São Paulo, according to Crosslam, it is the tallest building of its type in Brazil. 
Crosslam, which usually acts as an engineer for other firms, designed the building itself to demonstrate the potential of mass timber in Brazil.
“We believed that, to sell the first timber building, we first had to build our own,” said Crosslam founder José Alberto Corrêa. “Otherwise, the Brazilian market wouldn’t trust that it was possible.”
To further demonstrate the method’s viability, the building was constructed in four weeks by a five-person team, according to Crosslam. The timber was produced in the company’s factory using wood sourced from Brazil. 
Aurora 275 sits next to smaller residential buildings. It features a metal-clad facade, with yellow detailing running up its face. 
It extends in two directions from a concrete core, with balconies for each of the 10 apartments. 
On the outside, the timber elements are only visible on the undersides of the balconies. 
However, on the inside, the timber elements are more visible, with the cross-laminated timber floorplates and glulam beams exposed, giving the interiors a warm feel. 
Crosslam said that the structure shows the potential for timber buildings for industrial-style housing production in the country. 

            Read:
          
“Using engineered timber offers benefits related to the industrialisation of construction, the efficiency of the assembly process, and building performance,” said the project teaam. 
“The main advantages include: faster assembly, dimensional precision, reduced waste on the construction site, a lighter structure, and lower carbon emissions.”
The company claimed that the building is carbon neutral. 
Timber construction for tall buildings is gaining popularity across the world, with inroads into construction in North America and Europe. The current tallest timber structure in the world recently topped out in Australia.
